# Introduction

In Indian astrology, Kerala Astrology is commonly practiced in North India and especially in South India. Its original source is the region of Kerala. The material published so far under the name of Kerala Astrology is of three types. The first is **Numerical Astrology** <note>original: "Aksharanka Jyotish" - a system where letters are assigned numerical values</note>, the second is **Body-Touch Science** <note>original: "Anga Sparsha Vidya" - predicting based on which body part a person touches</note>, and the third is **Subjective Horoscope Astrology**. Among these, the curiosity of astrologers and the general public is greatest toward the first two.

**The Antiquity of Kerala Astrology—**It is nearly impossible to estimate how old Kerala Astrology is. We can only say that, like other sciences, the blossoming and development of Indian astrology has occurred since time immemorial. Ancient astrologers have given its subject matter a place in their texts. Durgadeva, a scholar of astrology in South India, gave eight types of "Question-Afflictions" <note>original: "Prashnarishta" - omens or obstacles identified at the time a question is asked</note> in his book *Rishtu Samuccaya* in 1032 AD: (1) Finger question, (2) Lac-dye question, (3) Yellow-pigment question, (4) Letter-of-the-question, (5) Embraced question, (6) Burnt question, (7) Blazing question, and (8) Peaceful question. This book also provides the method for answering these questions. This book titled *Rishtu Samuccaya*, composed by Durgadeva, is written in the Prakrit language. At its conclusion, the teacher writes:

> "Durgadeva composed this *Rishtu Samuccaya* scripture, which draws upon the meaning of many scriptures, by the word of Sanyamadeva." <note>original: "Raiyam bahu satthattham uvajivita hu Duggaevena. Rindhu samuccaya sattham vayanena Sanjama devassa." - translated from Prakrit</note>

The name of Durgadeva's teacher was Sanyamadeva. He was also an expert in the Kerala sciences. From this, the conclusion can be drawn that one thousand years ago, the Kerala system was widely popular in India.

**The Foundation of Kerala Astrology—**The fundamental basis of Kerala Astrology is the astrological signals expressed through a person's psychological movements. The feelings present in a person's inner conscience are revealed through their speech and physical gestures. A person's various facial expressions and gestures indicate the tendencies of their inner mind. Whatever auspicious or inauspicious thought arises in a human mind, its result is manifested accordingly. Kerala Astrology predicts results only after observing the reflection of those auspicious or inauspicious thoughts. So be it.

By asking the seeker to name a flower, fruit, deity, or river, and using the first letter of that name, or the first letter of the question, or the sum of the numerical values of the collection of letters, the knowledge of questions regarding the three periods of time <note>past, present, and future</note> is obtained. In the **Income-Knowledge** <note>original: "Aaya Gyana" - a specific calculation method</note> method, work is accomplished using only the first letter. In the **Constant-Number** <note>original: "Dhruvanka"</note> method, calculations are performed using all the letters of the question. In the **Body-Touch Science**, the auspicious or inauspicious future is determined on the basis of the person's gestures...

<summary>This page introduces Kerala Astrology, its three main branches, and its historical roots dating back at least to the 11th-century scholar Durgadeva. It explains that the system is based on psychological cues, gestures, and numerical values assigned to letters.</summary>
<keywords>Kerala Astrology, Durgadeva, Rishtu Samuccaya, Numerical Astrology, Body-Touch Science, Sanyamadeva, divination methods, Indian astrology history</keywords>

<meta>This page serves as the introduction and preface to the "Compendium of Kerala Horary Astrology," establishing the historical context and the translator's methodology.</meta>

<page-num>4</page-num>
<header>Compendium of Kerala Horary Astrology</header>

It is stated that it appears the current craze for Numerology <note>original: "Ankajyotish"</note> in the Western world has its original source in the Kerala Astrology of India.

**About the Present Work —** The present work is a compendium. There is a state of uncertainty regarding the author of this book; however, because the phrase "**Spoken by Muladeva**" <note>original: "Muladevena Bhashitam"</note> is attached in several places throughout the text, it can be believed that a scholar named Muladeva compiled it. The first half of the book could certainly be the composition of Muladeva. There are significant textual variations <note>original: "pathabheda"</note> between the copies published from two different locations and the material obtained from other references. Errors are also plentiful. Therefore, after thoroughly correcting the text, a Hindi commentary named **Richa** has been composed along with the prose-order <term>Anvaya: the logical connection of words in a verse to make the meaning clear</term>. In the commentary, the clarification of profound subjects has been done well. Through many tables, this work is presented to the readers in a way that is useful for students and the general public. The style of the commentary is not pedantic but is suited to the present time.

**Composition Date of the Work —** Like the author of this work, its date of composition is also unknown. However, this book is extremely useful for everyone from ordinary astrologers to the general public. It does not contain complex mathematical processes. For an astrologer to obtain accurate answers through this, it is necessary for them to be a believer in the divine <term>Astika: one who accepts the authority of the Vedas or the existence of a higher reality</term>, discerning, and of virtuous conduct.

->With a request for forgiveness from the learned scholars.<-

Friday, the seventh day of the bright half of the month of Chaitra }
Vikram Era 2059 <note>Approximately 2002 CE</note> }
Punarvasu <note>The seventh lunar mansion or Nakshatra</note> }

Servant of the learned,
Acharya Pandit Guruprasad Gaur

->|| Fortune ||<-

# Compendium of Kerala Horary Astrology
## (First Volume)

### || The Author’s Invocation ||

> I bow to the Sage of Kerala, who, through divine vision, proclaimed the matters of the three periods of time <note>past, present, and future</note> for the sake of understanding the fruits of the three worlds. || 1 ||

**Syntax** — To that Sage of Kerala, who by divine eye proclaimed the subjects of the three times for the knowledge of the results of the three worlds, I offer my salutation. || 1 ||

**Richa English Commentary** — Salutations to the great Sage Kerala, who, having perceived through his divine vision, described the subjects of inquiries related to the three times—past, future, and present—so that the auspicious and inauspicious fruits of the three worlds may be known.

<note>The Sage Kerala is considered the founder of the Kerala school of astrology. Therefore, the author has offered obeisance to him in this opening invocation.</note>

### || The Commentator’s Invocation ||

> Having bowed to Shiva and Parvati <note>original: "Shivā-shivam," referring to the divine couple</note>, to Ganesha, the Lord of Hosts, and to Saraswati, the Goddess of Learning, I compose this English commentary for the quick understanding of horary inquiries. || 1 ||
> This science, known as Kerala Horary Astrology, which was well-collected by Muladeva—having edited and refined the original text—is presented by the grace of the Guru and for the pleasure of the Guru. || 2 ||
> Having obtained the lamp of knowledge, having fashioned a wick out of excellent letters, and having joined it with the oil of vowels, one should light it with the fuel of answers. || 2 ||

**Syntax** — Having obtained the lamp of knowledge, making a wick of good letters, and joining it with the oil of vowels, one should ignite it with the fuel of answers. || 2 ||

**Richa Commentary** — Taking up the lamp in the form of knowledge, creating a wick out of the best characters/letters, filling that lamp with the oil <note>or ghee</note> of vowels, one should then kindle it with the fuel of the answers provided to the inquiries.

### || Now, the Purpose of the Inquiry ||

> By the astrologer <term>Daivajña: one who knows the divine or fate</term>, with the desire to know good and bad results of destiny, every mortal is inevitably brought to the Kerala system. || 3 ||
> In ancient times, even Brahma, the Lord of the World, when approaching Lord Vishnu for the sake of knowledge, listened to the discourse regarding the determination of horary inquiries. || 4 ||

**Syntax** — By the astrologer, through the desire for the good and bad fruits of destiny, everything is necessarily brought into the Kerala (science). || 3 ||

<summary>This introductory page establishes the divine lineage of the Kerala school of astrology, offering prayers to Sage Kerala and deities before explaining that even the creator god Brahma relied on this system to understand destiny.</summary>
<keywords>Kerala Jyotish, Horary Astrology, Mangalacharana, Sage Kerala, Muladeva, divination, Brahma and Vishnu</keywords>

--- Page 11 ---
<meta>This page continues the introductory discourse on the Kerala system of divination, establishing its divine lineage and the ethical requirements for practitioners.</meta>

<page-num>2</page-num>
->Compendium of Kerala Horary Science<-

In ancient times, for the sake of knowledge (of the Kerala system), even Brahma, the Lord of the World, approached Vishnu to gain understanding and listened to his words. || 4 ||

**Richa Commentary**—An astrologer is called a <term>Daivajña</term> <note>literally "knower of fate"</note>. One who knows the *daiva* (the fruits of past actions or destiny) is a Daivajña; if such a person desires to know the auspicious and inauspicious results of life, they must certainly study the <term>Kerala Shastra</term> <note>A specific system of horary astrology or "Prashna" associated with the Kerala tradition</note>. Through this, a person attains complete information. In ancient times, even Brahma, the Lord of the World, went to Lord Vishnu to hear this Kerala system for deciding questions and outcomes. || 3-4 ||

In these verses, while explaining the importance of the Kerala system, it is said that through this, the fruits of <term>Prarabdha</term> <note>destiny currently being experienced</note>, <term>Sanchita</term> <note>accumulated past karma</note>, and <term>Kriyaman</term> <note>actions being performed now</note> can be easily understood. Therefore, it is essential for an astrologer to study it. One should accept this knowledge from wherever it may be obtained; for even being the Lord of the World (the Creator) himself, Brahma learned this knowledge from Lord Vishnu. In ancient times, memory was so sharp that everything was memorized after hearing it just once; this is called <term>Shruti Parampara</term> <note>the oral tradition of learning by hearing</note>. For this reason, the Vedas and Upanishads are called *Shruti*. Knowledge is attained by approaching the Knower with service and without ego. Kabir Sahib has said—"If one finds a Guru even at the cost of one's head, consider it a bargain."

### || Now, regarding those who are fit and unfit for asking questions ||

> If the answer is given to the mean, the hypocritical, the wicked, the faithless, or the mocker, it will not come true even if Lord Shiva himself were to speak it. || 5 ||
> An answer to a question should never be given in a public assembly, nor at night. It should not be given in the afternoon, nor to an untrustworthy person, and never in haste. || 6 ||
> If the astrologer does not provide guidance to a devotee or to one who is suffering and humble, his knowledge becomes fruitless; therefore, he should always speak to them. || 7 ||

**Prose Order (Anvaya):**—Even if Shambhu (Shiva) himself speaks, the answer given to the mean, the hypocrite, the wicked, and the faithless mocker does not attain truth. || 5 ||
An answer to a question should not be spoken in an assembly, nor at night, nor in the afternoon; and to an untrustworthy person, it should never be spoken in haste. || 6 ||
If the astrologer does not give direction to a devotee or a distressed and humble person, then it becomes useless; therefore, he should always speak to them. || 7 ||

**Richa Commentary**—If the <term>Prichchhaka</term> <note>the querent or person asking the question</note> is mean, a hypocrite, wicked, or a faithless mocker, the answer given to them will not prove accurate, even if it were delivered by Lord Shankar (Shiva) himself. One should not answer a question in a large group of people. Similarly, one should not answer questions at night. One should also not give an answer during the afternoon (after midday) or to an untrustworthy querent. Answers to questions should never be given in a hurried manner. However, if the querent...

--- Page 12 ---
<meta>This page continues the discussion on the ethical conduct of an astrologer and the specific conditions under which a consultation should or should not take place.</meta>

<page-num>3</page-num>
<header>First Half</header>

If the seeker is a devotee, humble, or in distress, then the astrologer's knowledge becomes fruitless if they do not answer the question. Therefore, the question of such a person must certainly be answered. || 5-7 ||

In these three verses, the qualifications and disqualifications of the questioner are indicated. Those who are wicked in their conduct are called "vile persons." Those who have flaws in their conduct but wear a cloak of goodness on the outside are called "hypocrites" <note>original: *pakhandi*</note>; those who are cunning are called "shrewd." For those who have no faith in astrology, ancient knowledge, or the scriptures composed by the Sages, and who make fun of all these (the mockers)—the answer given to their questions does not turn out to be correct; because such people come only to test the astrologer or with the intent of mocking them.

The consultation of an astrologer, a physician, and a lawyer is confidential, but the person to whom the question relates is either the seeker <term>Prichchhaka: the person asking the question</term> themselves or a person or object related to them. Therefore, answering a question in front of others violates that confidentiality, which may cause some kind of harm to the seeker. Suppose a person is contesting a general election for the Central or Provincial Legislature; they are asking a question about their victory or defeat in the election, and according to the scriptures, the answer is received in the form of defeat. Now, if the astrologer announces that answer in front of many people (in an assembly), then that information may reach the seeker's rival, which will undoubtedly harm the seeker; because this will increase the rival's morale. For this reason, the astrologer is cautioned:—"It should never be spoken in an assembly." <note>original Latin/Sanskrit equivalent: *Sabhyayam naiva vaktavyam*</note>

**Prohibition of Nighttime for Declaring Answers**—Firstly, the twilight hour and then the early evening <note>original: *pradosh-vela*, the period just after sunset</note> are prohibited for answering questions. Even after this, the nighttime is such that the astrologer <term>Daivagya: one who knows the divine/fate; an astrologer</term> is tired from the day's work, and thus at that time, their mind cannot function properly. Therefore, do not answer questions at night. Similarly, in the afternoon period after the midday meal, there is a tendency toward rest and laziness; therefore, there is a possibility of error in the answer.

**Untrustworthy Persons**—An untrustworthy person refers to a questioner whom you consider suspicious. Thieves, pickpockets, smugglers, and persons with anti-social or criminal backgrounds are untrustworthy. They can misuse the astrologer's answer after asking a question. Therefore, a complete stranger should be thoroughly examined to see if they fall into the category of the untrustworthy. Suppose a person is in the business of theft; now if they go to the astrologer and ask whether their "work" will be successful or not—without even disclosing the nature of the work—then in the event of the astrologer saying "there is a combination for success in work," that person will commit theft with greater enthusiasm. The amount of suffering this would cause to the victim or victims...

-># Collection of Kerala Horary Astrology<-

It is difficult to imagine. Similarly, in a situation where a seeker asks for the name of a thief, if the answer is accurate and the seeker reveals the astrologer's name to the thief (even if the goods are recovered by the police), enmity with the thief is certain. Therefore, such a seeker is also considered "untrustworthy" because they have breached confidentiality. Thus, anyone who might break the secrecy of the answer or misuse the prediction is categorized as untrustworthy. There can be many other types of untrustworthy individuals; therefore, an astrologer should always avoid answering their questions after careful consideration and must remain cautious about protecting confidentiality themselves—this is the intended meaning.

"**Never in haste**" <note>original: "tvaritaṃ na kadācana"</note>—The answer to a query should only be given after thorough reflection; otherwise, an answer given in a hurry or rush will certainly be erroneous, bringing ill-repute to the astrologer.

Those suffering from disease, debt, or distress are called the "wretched" and the "afflicted." Along with these, those who hold a sense of reverence for the astrologer but are poor must be answered by the <term>Diviner (Daivajña)</term> <note>A knower of fate or a learned astrologer</note>; otherwise, the astrologer will incur the curse of <term>Saraswati</term> <note>The Goddess of knowledge and speech</note> and their knowledge will perish.

### || Now, the Rules for the Seeker ||

> Therefore, a King, holding flowers, gems, and fruits in his hands,
> Having bowed in the morning and facing the East,
> Should gather astrologers skilled in the limbs of Horoscopy and well-wishers,
> And ask them all at once. || 8 ||
> Having worshipped the planets along with their limbs and the astrologer devoted to his duties,
> A man, full of faith, with full hands and a calm mind, should inquire. || 9 ||
> He who inquires of the astrologer while offering fruits and flowers,
> To him alone should the answer be told; then it becomes true, not otherwise. || 10 ||

**Prose Order (Anvaya):** Therefore, a King with flowers, gems, and fruits in his hands, having gathered astrologers who are skilled in the limbs of horoscopy and are well-wishers, should sit facing East in the morning, bow, and ask them all at once. || 8 || Having worshipped the planets (with their parts) and the astrologer who is devoted to his own rituals, a man who is undisturbed, with full hands and filled with faith, should ask. || 9 || To him who asks the astrologer while possessing fruits and flowers, the answer to the question should be told; otherwise, truth is not possible. || 10 ||

**Commentary:** Thus, a King or any seeker should sit facing East in the morning, gather astrologers proficient in the branches of <term>Horoscopy (Hora-shastra)</term> and those who wish him well, offer salutations, and...

<header>First Half</header>

The seeker should ask their question only once. One should approach the <term>astrologer<note>original: "daivajña" — one who knows the divine or fate</note></term> only while carrying offerings such as fruits, flowers, or gems in their hands.

With a peaceful mind and full hands, one should devotedly question the astrologer, who is dedicated to his own spiritual duties along with the study of the nine planets and their limbs.

When a person asks a question of the astrologer while offering fruits and flowers, the answer to the question turns out to be true; otherwise, it does not. || 8-10 ||

In these verses, the phrases "ask only once" <note>original: "sakṛdeva pṛcchet"</note> and "with full hands" <note>original: "pūrṇapāṇiḥ"</note> are extremely important. "With full hands" means that one should ask a question only after taking fruits, flowers, grain, gems, or currency notes according to one's capacity; otherwise, the result of the query will not be accurate. Similarly, "ask only once" means the question should be posed a single time and not repeated over and over. Secondly—one should not ask more than one question on a single occasion.

## || Rules Regarding the Direction of the Seeker ||

->East, West, Northeast, and North are auspicious directions. South is demonic and wicked, while Southeast and Northwest are void. || 11 ||<-

**Prose Order:** East, West, North, and Northeast directions are auspicious. South is demonic and wicked, while Southeast and Northwest are void. || 11 ||

**Commentary:** If the person asking the question sits to the East, West, or North of the astrologer, the result is auspicious. Sitting in the South or Southwest results in "wicked" (inauspicious) outcomes, and sitting in the Southeast or Northwest should be said to yield "void" results. || 11 ||

Here, "void result" <note>original: "shūnya phala"</note> implies that there will be no significant outcome, good or bad—meaning a mediocre or neutral result is obtained. For the ease of the readers, the directions are presented along with their presiding deities and results. Here, eight directions are recognized: four cardinal directions and four corner directions. The corners are also called <term>intermediate directions<note>original: "vidishā"</note></term>.

| 1 | 2 | 3 | 4 | 5 | 6 | 7 | 8 | Serial No. |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| East | E + S | South | S + W | West | W + N | North | N + E | Name of 8 Directions |
| Prācī | Āgneyī (SE) | Avācī (S) | Nairṛtya (SW) | Pratīcī (W) | Vāyavya (NW) | Udīcī (N) | Īśāna (NE) | Synonyms |
| Indra | Agni | Yama | Demon | Varuṇa | Vāyu | Kubera | Shiva | Presiding Deity |
| Auspicious | Void | Wicked | Wicked | Auspicious | Void | Auspicious | Auspicious | Result |

# Page 6
## A Compendium of Kerala Horary Astrology

There is often not enough space for a seeker to sit in any of the eight directions to ask a question; in such cases, determining the result based on direction becomes a practical difficulty. The solution to this problem is that the astrologer should prepare a diagram, as shown below, on paper, cardboard, or a wooden pedestal. The seeker should be asked to place the ring finger <note>Sanskrit: anamika</note> of their right hand upon this diagram. The direction should be decided based on which spot the seeker places their finger. It should be noted that the central square in this diagram belongs to the astrologer; the seeker should not place their finger there. For convenience, the astrologer's (central) portion of the diagram should be colored yellow or another color, and the seeker should be instructed before placing their finger to choose any of the remaining eight squares, excluding the colored central part.

->|| Direction-Indicating Diagram ||<-

| Northeast | East | Southeast |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| North | **Place for the Astrologer <term>Daivajña: one who knows the divine/fate</term> to sit** | South |
| Northwest | West | Southwest |

->|| Now, the Auspicious Omens at the Time of Inquiry ||<-

> If, during the inquiry, the sight or sound of auspicious objects is pleasing to the eyes and the mind, one should predict a favorable outcome. || 12 ||
> If, at the time of the query, the sound of horses, elephants, bulls, or swans is heard, or if they are seen, one should declare the query to have a successful result. || 13 ||
<note>Original Sanskrit: "Dṛṅmanasoḥ prītikaraṃ praśneṣu darśanaṃ yadi śravaṇam..."</note>

**Prose Order:** If in inquiries the sight or hearing of auspicious objects is pleasing to the eyes and mind, one should indicate auspiciousness there. If at the time of inquiry there is the sight or the sound of horses, elephants, bulls, swans, etc., then one should indicate the query as granting a good result.

**Commentary:** If at the time of the question, one sees or hears of auspicious items (such as yogurt, milk, a cow with its calf, a pot filled with water, etc.), then the result of the query is auspicious. Similarly, if at that time the sound of a horse, elephant, bull, ox, or swan is heard, or if they are seen, the result of the query is also auspicious. || 12-13 ||

In these verses, the happiness of the mind is called the greatest omen. When the mind is joyful, all tasks are accomplished; as stated in other texts—

-> "The satisfaction of the mind itself is the supreme sign of victory." and <-
<note>Original Latin-script Sanskrit: "Manasastuṣṭirevātra paramaṃ jayalakṣaṇam."</note>
-> "Eagerness of mind and the thrill of the heart signify the gain of auspiciousness and a flow of victory." <-

Regarding auspicious <term>Shakunas</term><note>omens or signs</note>, the scripture states:
> “The sound of 'Swasti' <note>well-being</note>, 'Vriddhi' <note>prosperity</note>, the Nandyavarta <note>a sacred labyrinthine symbol</note>, and the Kaustubha <note>a divine jewel</note>; the auspicious, deep, and captivating sound of musical instruments.”

That is to say: deities, friends, Vedic-reciting Brahmins, a blazing fire, a courtesan, Durva grass, fresh cow dung, gold, silver, copper, all gemstone materials, medicines (herbs), astrologers, barley, white mustard, a sword, a vessel, a banner, earth, weapons, the throne and all royal insignia, a corpse free from weeping, ghee, yogurt, milk, fruits, dry fruits, the resounding of the words 'Swasti' and 'Vriddhi', the Kaustubha gem, the conch shell, the sounds of auspicious musical instruments, the sound of songs sung in the three musical notes of Gandhara, Shadja, and Rishabha, and even a gust of warm wind carrying dust—all these things are said to remove all obstacles.

### || The Eight Types of Inquiry ||

> Combined, Uncombined, Struck, Unstruck, Striking, 
> Embraced, Smoked, and Burnt are the types of inquiry. || 14 ||

**Connection:** Simple. || 14 ||

**Commentary:** In the <term>Kerala Shastra</term><note>A specific tradition of South Indian astrology and divination</note>, there are eight types of <term>Prashna</term><note>inquiry or divination based on the moment a question is asked</note>: (1) Combined, (2) Uncombined, (3) Struck, (4) Unstruck, (5) Striking, (6) Embraced, (7) Smoked, and (8) Burnt. The characteristics and definitions of each of these are explained hereafter. || 14 ||

### || The Combined Inquiry ||

> If the inquirer asks the question while touching their own body at the time of the inquiry, 
> then it is a 'Combined Inquiry,' and it results in gain. || 15 ||

**Connection:** Simple. || 15 ||

**Commentary:** If the <term>Prashta</term><note>the person asking the question</note> asks a question while touching their own body at the time of the inquiry, it is called a "Combined Inquiry" (<term>Samyukta Prashna</term>). A Combined Inquiry is an indicator of profit or success. || 15 ||

The specialty of the Combined Inquiry is that the person asking the question uses their right hand to—

--- Page 17 ---
<meta>This page continues the technical analysis of the querent's physical gestures and circumstances during a divination session, specifically focusing on the "Unconnected" and "Afflicted" types of queries.</meta>

# Compendium of Kerala Horary Science
<note>original: "Kerala-Prashna-Shastra-Sangraha"</note>

...touches any part of one's own body. Then it is an indicator of special gain. If one touches the rest of the body with the left hand, excluding the left limbs, then the possibility of gain should be considered negligible. In the original <term>Mula</term><note>The foundational root text</note>, there is no specific mention of left or right. Therefore, since the right hand is predominant, it is appropriate to interpret the touching as being done with that hand.

> If the querent asks while lying on the road, or while mounted on a swing, elephant, or horse;
> If they ask without devotion, or without offering fruit or wealth, then it is an "Unconnected Query" <term>Asamyukta-Prashna</term>.
> In such a query, gain and happiness occur only after many days. || 16 ||

**Prose Order:** Simple. || 16 ||

**Commentary:** If the person asking the question <term>Prashta</term> asks while walking on a path, or while lying in their bedroom or on a bed; or if they ask while swinging in a hammock, riding in a palanquin, in a lift, on stairs, or while traveling on an elephant, horse, camel, mule, chariot, bullock cart, bicycle, motor vehicle, or train; if they are devoid of feeling <term>Bhava</term>, or lack the customary offering of fruit or money <term>Dakshina</term>—then a question asked under such circumstances is "Unconnected." In this type of query, profit, happiness, and the fulfillment of the task occur only after a long duration.

**Clarification:** The eight types of queries discussed here clearly demonstrate the psychological background of the seeker. While a "Connected Query" <term>Samyukta-Prashna</term> indicates a balanced mindset, an "Unconnected Query" reveals an unbalanced and disorganized state of mind. In this situation, the person asking is careless and irresponsible toward their goal. There is a lack of mutual cooperation between their soul, mind, and senses, and they ask the question with only half a heart. The effort made by them is also half-hearted. Consequently, the delay in the success of the work occurs in proportion to the laxity of their effort.

### || The Afflicted Query ||
<note>original: "Abhihata Prashna"</note>

> If at the time of the query, the seeker touches the left side of the body with the left hand;
> Then it is an "Afflicted Query," which results in no gain. || 17 ||

**Prose Order:** Simple. || 17 ||

**Commentary:** When, at the time of the query, the person asking touches the left part of their own body with their left hand, it is called an **Afflicted Query** <term>Abhihata Prashna</term>; its result is unprofitable. || 17 ||

The word "Abhihata" is an adjective formed by adding the prefix "Abhi" to the root "Han" <note>to strike or kill</note> and the suffix "Kta." The meaning of this word is—beaten, wounded, afflicted, or hurt. Therefore, in this type of query, one should understand that "the fruit of the question has been struck down."

### || The Unstruck Question ||

**If the querent touches another person's body with their own hand,**
**then the question is known as "Unstruck" <note>Sanskrit: Anabhihata; meaning unimpeded or not struck down</note>, and it is remembered as bringing success in the task. || 18 ||**

Word-for-word connection — If the querent touches another's body with their hand, then the question is remembered as "Unstruck," which brings success in the task. || 18 ||

Commentary — When the seeker asks a question while touching another person's body with their left hand, the astrologer <term>Daivajña: one who knows the divine or a professional astrologer</term> should recognize that question as "Unstruck." The result of this question is the attainment of the desired goal. || 18 ||

In this type of question, because the fruit of the action is not "struck" <note>meaning there is no negative interference</note>, the work is accomplished.

### || The Afflicted Question ||

**If one rubs the head, the heart, the feet, the hands, or the waist,**
**then it is an "Afflicted" <note>Sanskrit: Abhighātika; meaning striking or injurious</note> question, causing distress to the querent. || 19 ||**

Word-for-word connection — (If the seeker) rubs the head, heart, feet, hand, or waist, (then) it is an "Afflicted" question (which) causes distress to the seeker. || 19 ||

Commentary — When the querent asks a question while rubbing (massaging) their own head, heart, feet, hands, or waist, it is called an "Afflicted" question. This brings grief and distress to the person asking. || 19 ||

Striking or rubbing the limbs is considered a bad omen <term>Apashakuna: an inauspicious sign or portent</term>. Cracking the knuckles also falls into this category. These signs indicate the mental anxiety of the seeker and future troubles. The word "Abhighāti" also means an enemy. In this type of question, the work is obstructed by some enemy.

### || The Embraced Question ||

**If the seeker touches their own right limb with their right hand,**
**then the "Embraced" <note>Sanskrit: Āliṅgita</note> question results in profit, happiness, and so on. || 20 ||**

Word-for-word connection — Simple. || 20 ||

Commentary — When the seeker asks a question at the time of consultation while touching any right part of their own body with their right hand, then that question, named "Embraced," brings profit and happiness. || 20 ||

In this type of question, one gains friends, sons, a wife, or other well-wishers, and the work is completed through their cooperation.

### || The Smoky Question ||

**If the seeker, with either the right or left hand,**
**touches their entire body, then it is a "Smoky" <note>Sanskrit: Abhidhūmita; implying clouded or obscured</note> question.**
**In this question, there is a little profit and the arrival of friends and others. || 21 ||**

<summary>This page details four types of omens based on the querent's physical gestures—Unstruck, Afflicted, Embraced, and Smoky—which predict the success or failure of their inquiry. It emphasizes how subconscious movements like rubbing the head or touching limbs serve as portents for the astrologer.</summary>

# Compendium of Kerala Horary Astrology
<page-num>10</page-num>

**Connection:** If the seeker touches their entire body with either the right or the left hand, then the query is classified as "Smoky" (*Abhidhumita*). In this query, there may be the arrival of friends and a small amount of profit. || 21 ||

**Commentary:** When a seeker touches many parts of their body, or more than one limb, with either their right or left hand while asking a question, it is called a Smoky Query. In this situation, a small profit is obtained, and there is a brief meeting with friends, relatives, or loved ones. || 21 ||

<note>The original Sanskrit text mentions "touching all limbs" (Sarvanga-sparsha). Since a person cannot literally touch every part of their body simultaneously, a student of this subject might find it difficult to make a judgment. Therefore, "touching all limbs" should be understood as the seeker continuously moving or sliding their hand over various parts of their body while asking the question. The term "Smoky" (Abhidhumita) implies being surrounded by smoke; this gesture is so named because the hand moves restlessly over the body like drifting smoke.</note>

### || The Burnt Query ||

->If the seeker asks while weeping, in sorrow, or stricken by fear, or near a lowly place;<-
->Or if they ask without a spirit of devotion, then it is declared a Burnt Query.<-
->This query results in great grief, distress, sorrow, and pain.<- || 22 ||

**Connection:** Simple/Direct. || 22 ||

**Commentary:** If the seeker asks a question while crying, terrified, or in a state of grief; or if they ask the question in or near a "lowly place" <note>original: "Nicha-sthala"</note> (such as a hospital, post-mortem room, cremation ground, battlefield, accident site, deep pit, basement, slaughterhouse, or a foul-smelling area); or if they ask without any sense of reverence, then the query asked under these conditions is called <term>Burnt (Dagdha)</term> <note>Dagdha refers to a state where the potential of the question has been "incinerated" by negative omens.</note>. This type of query brings mourning, agony, suffering, and significant loss. || 22 ||

<note>The word "Dagdha" means burnt. The gestures made by the seeker in this instance indicate that the "seeds" of effort sown for the task have been scorched; they will not bear fruit. Due to failure, one will have to endure intense despair and pain.</note>

### || The Greatness of the Kerala System ||

Now, this science illuminates questions regarding the three periods of time (past, present, and future), including matters of living beings, minerals, and plants <note>original: "Jiva-Dhatu-Mula"</note>; the location of lost items and whether they will be recovered; imprisonment and liberation; life and death; victory and defeat; profit and loss; and arrivals or departures. This science is never otherwise (it is unfailing). || 23 ||

**Connection:** Simple/Direct. || 23 ||

**Commentary:** Through this science, one gains knowledge of questions related to living beings, metals/minerals, and botanical roots. One learns whether a lost object will be found, its current location, matters of captivity and release, life and death, victory and defeat, profit and loss, and coming and going. The light of this scripture makes the answers to these subjects visible to the eye. || 23 ||

<page-num>11</page-num>

Almost all problems of human life fall under these mentioned subjects of inquiry.

## || The Result of the Querent's Gaze ||

> By looking upward, it is a Living Being; by looking downward, it is a Root.
> By looking straight ahead, it is a Mineral; so it was spoken by Muladeva. || 24 ||

**Prose Order** — By an upward gaze, a Living Being; and by a downward gaze, a Root; by a level gaze, it is a Mineral. (Thus) it was spoken by Muladeva <note>Muladeva is a legendary figure in Sanskrit literature, often associated with cunning, arts, and divination</note>. || 24 ||

**Commentary** — In this verse, Muladeva, while explaining the result of the seeker's gaze, says that if the person asking the question looks upward at the time of the inquiry, then their question is related to a "Living Being" <term>Jiva: refers to humans, animals, or deities</term>. If the seeker's gaze is downward (toward the ground), then the question is related to a "Root" <term>Mula: refers to plants, vegetables, or botanical matters</term>. However, if the seeker looks neither up nor down but looks straight ahead, then in such a "level gaze," the question is related to a "Mineral" <term>Dhatu: refers to metals, ores, or inanimate objects</term>. || 24 ||

If the seeker's neck is straight while asking the question, it is a "level gaze." If the neck is tilted back, it is an "upward gaze." Conversely, if the seeker's neck is tilted forward, it is called a "downward gaze."

## || Chart of Inquiry Subjects According to the Seeker's Gaze ||

| Upward Gaze | Downward Gaze | Level Gaze | Seeker's Gaze |
| :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- |
| Looking Upward | Looking Downward | Looking Straight | Characteristic |
| Living Being (Jiva) | Root (Mula) | Mineral (Dhatu) | Subject of Inquiry |

## || Knowledge of the Inquiry by Dividing the Day into Three Parts ||

> Having divided the duration of the day by three at the time of the seeker's inquiry:
> In the rising portion, one should know the inquiry is "Embraced." || 25 ||
> In the middle portion of the day's duration, the inquiry is "Smoky."
> In the setting portion of the day, the inquiry is then "Burnt." || 26 ||

**Prose Order** — Having divided the duration of the day at the time of inquiry by three: in the rising part, know the inquiry to be "Embraced" <note>original: alingita</note>. In the middle part of the day's duration, the inquiry is "Smoky" <note>original: abhidhumita</note>. If the inquiry is in the setting part of the day, it is then "Burnt" <note>original: dagdha</note>. || 25-26 ||

**Commentary** — Divide the local duration of the day <note>Dinaman: the length of time from sunrise to sunset</note> on which the question is asked by three, and then see in which part of the day the question was posed. If the desired time of the inquiry...

# Compendium of Kerala Horary Astrology

If the query is made during the rising portion (the beginning) of the day, it should be known as an **Embraced** (*Alingita*) query. If the query is made during the middle portion of the day, understand it to be **Smoky** (*Abhidhumita*). If the query is made during the final portion of the day (the setting portion), then it is a **Burnt** (*Dagdha*) query. || 25-26 ||

Suppose a diviner calculates the time of a query based on the local sunrise. The desired time (*Ishta Kala*) <note>The time elapsed since sunrise</note> is 8 *ghatis* and 30 *palas*, and the total duration of daylight (*Dinamana*) for that day is 32 *ghatis* and 6 *palas*. By dividing this daylight duration by three, we obtain a quotient of 10 *ghatis* and 42 *palas*. Since the petitioner's query occurred within the first part of the day (the rising part), it is an **"Embraced Query."** If, on that same day, the time of the query had been between 10 *ghatis* 43 *palas* and 21 *ghatis* 24 *palas*, the query would fall into the **"Smoky"** category due to being in the middle portion. If the time of the query on that day was from 21 *ghatis* 25 *palas* to 32 *ghatis* 6 *palas*, it would be called a **"Burnt"** query. In this manner, the knowledge of the three "times" (*velas*) is gained through these three divisions of daylight.

->॥ Chart of the Three Times of Day ॥<-

| First Part (1) | Second Part (2) | Third Part (3) | Divisions of Day |
|:---|:---|:---|:---|
| Rising Part | Middle Part | Setting Part | Synonym |
| Rising Time | Middle Time | Setting Time | |
| Embraced Query | Smoky Query | Burnt Query | Type of Query |
| Embraced Time | Smoky Time | Burnt Time | Query Time |

---

->॥ Knowledge of Living Beings, Minerals, and Plants ॥<-

> The Living Being, Mineral, and Plant are to be divided by three in the rising stage.
> In the middle stage, Mineral, Plant, and Living Being are divided by three. || 27 ||
> In the setting stage of the day, know the Plant, Living Being, and Mineral.
> By dividing by three, one attains the easy-to-understand Kerala wisdom. || 28 ||
<note>original: "Jiva-dhatushcha mulancha..." These verses explain how to categorize the subject of a query—whether it concerns a person/animal (Jiva), a mineral/inanimate object (Dhatu), or a plant/root (Mula)—based on the sub-divisions of the day.</note>

**Prose Construction:** Dividing the rising period again by three, one finds the Living Being (*Jiva*), Mineral (*Dhatu*), and Plant (*Mula*). In the middle portion, divide by three again for Mineral, Plant, and Living Being. In the setting portion of the day, know the Plant, Living Being, and Mineral by dividing by three. This is the easy-to-understand Kerala wisdom. || 27-28 ||

---

->॥ Knowledge of Queries through Sub-divisions of the Three Times of Day ॥<-

| Rising Part | | | Middle Part | | | Setting Part | | | Time |
|:---:|:---:|:---:|:---:|:---:|:---:|:---:|:---:|:---:|:---:|
| First | Second | Third | First | Second | Third | First | Second | Third | Sub-time |
| Living Being | Mineral | Plant | Mineral | Plant | Living Being | Plant | Living Being | Mineral | Subject |

<note>The terms Jiva (Living Being), Dhatu (Mineral/Inanimate), and Mula (Plant/Root) represent the three fundamental categories of all things in the universe according to Vedic astrology.</note>

First Half

**Commentary:** Divide the initial part of the day—referred to as the "Rising Period"—into three equal sections. If the desired time of the query falls within the first section of this initial part, the question pertains to <term>Jiva</term> <note>Living beings, including humans and animals</note>. In the second section of the initial part, the question relates to <term>Dhatu</term> <note>Metals, minerals, or non-living materials</note>. In the third section, the subject of the query is <term>Mula</term> <note>Roots, plants, or vegetable matter</note>. Furthermore, in the three subdivisions of the middle part of the day, the subjects follow the order of Dhatu, Mula, and Jiva. In the three subdivisions of the final part of the day, the subjects follow the order of Mula, Jiva, and Dhatu. In this manner, the entire duration of the day is effectively divided into nine distinct segments.

### || Results of Queries in the Embraced Time ||

->In the Embraced time, an Embraced query yields an Embraced result. || 29 ||<-
->In the Embraced time, a Smoldering query yields a Smoldering result. || 30 ||<-
->In the Embraced time, a Burnt query yields a Burnt result. || 31 ||<-

**Prose Construction:** Simple. || 29-31 ||
**Commentary:** If an "Embraced" <note>original: "Alingita"; refers to a favorable or auspicious state of the breath or time</note> query is made during an Embraced time-period, the result is Embraced—meaning one attains profit, happiness, and honor. If a "Smoldering" <note>original: "Abhidhumita"; refers to a clouded or middling state</note> type of query is made during an Embraced time, the result is Smoldering—meaning happiness and profit are obtained only in small measures. If a "Burnt" <note>original: "Dagdha"; refers to an inauspicious or "consumed" state</note> query is made during an Embraced time, a Burnt result is obtained—meaning the outcome is inauspicious and painful. || 29-31 ||

### || Results of Queries in the Smoldering Time ||

->Now, in the Smoldering time, a Smoldering query yields an Embraced result. || 32 ||<-

->In the Smoldering time, an Embraced query yields a Burnt result. || 34 ||<-

**Prose Construction:** Simple. || 32-34 ||
**Commentary:** If a Smoldering query is made during the Smoldering time (the middle of the day), an Embraced result is obtained. If a Burnt query is made during a Smoldering time, a Smoldering result is obtained. If an Embraced query is made during a Smoldering time, a Burnt result is obtained. || 32-34 ||

### || Now, Predictions Based on a Chosen Number ||

> Double the chosen number and add one to it. 
> Divide that sum by three and predict the result based on the remainder. || 38 ||
> If the remainder is one, the concern is about living beings; if the remainder is two, it is declared as mineral/inanimate matter. 
> If the remainder is zero, one should know it concerns the botanical world, according to the words of Sharada <note>The Goddess of Wisdom/Saraswati</note>. || 39 ||

**Prose Order:** Double the chosen number. Add one to it. Divide that by three and predict the result from the remainder. If the remainder is one <note>original: "Chandra" or Moon, symbolizing the number 1</note>, it is a concern regarding living beings (*Jiva*). If the remainder is even <note>specifically 2</note>, it is a concern regarding minerals/metals (*Dhatu*). Just as the words of Sharada state, if the remainder is zero, know it to be a concern regarding plants/roots (*Mula*). || 38-39 ||

**Commentary:** Calculate the *Ishtakala* <note>the specific desired time or chosen number of the query</note> at the moment of the question. Double it (multiply by two) and to that...

<meta>This page continues the technical instructions for divination (Prashna Shastra), specifically focusing on determining the subject of a querent's thoughts based on mathematical calculations and physical observations.</meta>

<page-num>15</page-num>
<header>First Half</header>

Double the number and add one, then divide that by three and declare the result according to the remainder. If one remains, it is a concern regarding living beings <note>Sanskrit: Jiva-chinta; refers to humans, animals, or spirits</note>. If two remain, it is a concern regarding minerals <note>Sanskrit: Dhatu-chinta; refers to metals, inanimate objects, or money</note>. If zero or three remain, it should be known as a concern regarding roots <note>Sanskrit: Mula-chinta; refers to plants, agriculture, or vegetable matter</note>. This method is as true as the words of the Goddess Saraswati herself. || 38-39 ||

Suppose the desired time <term>Ishta Kala: the specific moment a question is asked</term> of the query is 17 <term>Ghati: a unit of time equal to 24 minutes</term> and 30 <term>Pal: a unit of time equal to 24 seconds</term>. Multiplying this by two gives 35. Adding one makes it 36. Dividing thirty-six by three leaves a remainder of zero. Therefore, according to the rule "know the root when zero remains," one should diagnose a concern regarding roots. Here, if the desired time had been 17 Ghati and 59 Pal, doubling it would yield 35 Ghati and 58 Pal, and adding one would result in 36 Ghati and 58 Pal. Since the division is performed only on the Ghati (hours/units), the remainder would still be zero and the answer would remain the same.

However, if the desired time were 18 Ghati and 0 Pal, then doubling it and adding one would result in the number 37. Dividing this by three leaves a remainder of one; then, according to "one remaining signifies the Moon/Living," one would have to declare a concern regarding living beings.

->|| Determining the Query Based on the Direction the Face is Turned ||<-

> If facing East, it is a concern of minerals; likewise, in the South, it is living beings.
> In the North, one should speak of roots; in the West, the result is mixed. || 40 ||

**Prose Order:** If the face is toward the East, there is concern for minerals, and likewise in the South for living beings. In the North-facing position, one should speak of roots. In the West, the result (is) mixed. || 40 ||

**Commentary:** If the seeker asks a question while facing East, then a concern for minerals should be stated, and if facing South, a concern for living beings. Similarly, a North-facing seeker has a concern for roots, but a West-facing seeker has a mixed concern involving minerals, roots, and living beings. The word 'Asya' means the mouth or face. || 40 ||

| :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- |
| Mineral Concern | Living Being Concern | Root Concern | Mixed Concern | Concern |

> By touching the arms, face, or jaw, the concern for living beings brings a good omen.
> By touching the heart or stomach, the concern for minerals is of middling result. || 42 ||
> By touching the anus or testicles, the concern for roots is of low or poor quality.
> By touching the knees, shanks, or feet, one should point to a concern for living beings. || 43 ||

# Compendium of Kerala Prasnashastra

**Connection of Words:** If the head is touched, it is a Living Being; if the feet are touched, it is a Plant; and if the middle of the body is touched, it is Mineral/Inanimate. This is according to the words of Sharada <note>The Goddess of Wisdom, Saraswati</note>. || 41 ||

Touching the arms, face, or head indicates a favorable inquiry into Living Beings. Touching the heart or stomach indicates an inquiry into Minerals/Inanimate objects of medium quality. || 42 ||

Touching the anus or scrotum indicates an inquiry into Plants/Roots of a low or unfavorable nature. Touching the knees, shins, or feet should be designated as an inquiry into Living Beings. || 43 ||

**Richa Commentary:** If the seeker asks a question while touching their own head, it is a <term>Jiva Chinta</term> <note>Inquiry regarding living beings, such as humans or animals</note>. By touching the feet, it is a <term>Mula Chinta</term> <note>Inquiry regarding "roots" or botanical life</note>. By touching the middle part of the body, it is a <term>Dhatu Chinta</term> <note>Inquiry regarding "minerals," metals, or inanimate objects</note>. This is the teaching of Saraswati. Touching the arms, mouth, or chin indicates a Living Being inquiry and yields a fortunate result. Touching the heart or stomach indicates a Mineral inquiry with moderate results. Touching the anus or testicles indicates a Plant inquiry of the lowest kind, yielding poor results. Touching the knees, calves, or the front of the feet should be considered a Living Being inquiry. || 41-43 ||

### ## Now, Predicting Results Based on Numbers

> Work is not seen in eight or two; with sixes and fours, one's work is accomplished.
> The numbers seven and three speak of patience; nine, one, and five speak of speed. || 44 ||

**Connection of Words:** In the numbers eight and two, the work is not seen <note>will not be successful</note>. Through the numbers "flavors" (six) and "oceans" (four), success in one's work occurs. The numbers three and seven speak of the need for patience. The numbers one, five, and nine speak of the work happening quickly. || 44 ||

**Richa Commentary:** In this verse, the result is explained according to <term>Anka Shastra</term> <note>Numerology</note>. The seeker should be asked to spontaneously pick any number from one to nine. If the number 2 or 8 comes from the seeker's mouth, the work will not be accomplished. If the number is 4 or 6, the work will be successful. If the seeker utters 3 or 7, the work will be delayed and the seeker must remain patient. If the seeker says 1, 5, or 9, the work will be completed very quickly. || 44 ||

# A Second Method for Answering Questions via Numbers—

The astrologer <note>term: Jyotishi; a practitioner of traditional Indian astrology and divination</note> should prepare a grid of nine squares, as shown to the side, on a piece of paper, cardboard, or a wooden pedestal. He should then ask the seeker <note>term: Prichchaka; the person asking the question</note> to place their finger on any single number of their choice. The finger must be placed clearly on a specific number. Placing the finger between two numbers or on a line will not yield a correct answer. The result of the numbers should be told to the seeker according to the table below.

| 1 | 2 | 3 |
|---|---|---|
| 6 | 5 | 4 |
| 7 | 8 | 9 |

### || Table of Numerical Results ||

| One | Two | Three | Four | Five | Six | Seven | Eight | Nine | **Number** |
| :---: | :---: | :---: | :---: | :---: | :---: | :---: | :---: | :---: | :---: |
| Immediate Success | Failure | Delay | Success | Swift Action | Success | Delayed Action | Failure | Swift Success | **Result** |

## || Now, the Method of Questioning via Letters ||

->In the forenoon, one should obtain the name of a flower from the mouth of a child. At midday, one should obtain the name of a fruit from the mouth of a young woman. In the afternoon, one should obtain the name of a tree from the mouth of an elder. Or, at night, the wise should obtain the name of a river from the mouth of anyone. <note>original Sanskrit: "Pūrvāhṇe bālakamukhāt puṣpanāma tu grāhayet..."</note> (Verses 45–46)<-

**Prose Construction:** The wise practitioner should have a child name a flower in the forenoon. At midday, have a young woman name a fruit. In the afternoon, have an elder name a tree. Or, if at night, have anyone name a river.

**Commentary:** If a question is asked during the forenoon (from sunrise until approximately 10:00 AM), one should have a child speak the name of any flower. If the time of the question is at midday (from 10:00 AM to 2:00 PM), one should have a young girl <note>The text uses 'kanya' implying a maiden or young woman</note> speak the name of a fruit. During the afternoon (from 2:00 PM until sunset), one should ask an elder for the name of a tree. If the question occurs at night, the name of a river should be taken from the mouth of any person (boy, girl, man, woman, or elder) to provide the answer to the query.

### || An Alternative Method ||

**Prose Construction:** In the morning, speak a flower; at midday, a fruit name. In the afternoon, a deity's name; and in the evening, speak of a river or stream.

**Commentary:** According to the opinion of some scholars, without considering the age of the speaker (child, elder, etc.), one should directly—

One should determine the answer to a query by having the consultant—or someone else—speak the name of a flower in the morning, the name of a deity at noon, and the name of a river in the evening. || 47 ||

In my opinion, this method is correct because this verse does not mention the nighttime, which is appropriate. If one were to take the name of a river at night according to verse 46, it would contradict the earlier verse 6, "No answers to queries at night," which explicitly prohibits giving divinatory answers during the night.

->OR<-

> The vowels and consonants of the sentences spoken by the consultant should be taken.
> If the letters of the query are too many or unclear, then the following method should be used. || 48 ||

**Syntax:** The construction is simple. || 48 ||

**Richa <note>Commentary</note>:** One should accept the letters of whatever sentence is received, including their vowels. If the letters of the query sentence are excessive or indistinct, then use the method described hereafter. || 48 ||

### || Method of Query According to the Consultant's Caste ||

> If the consultant is a Brahmin <note>the priestly class</note>, have them speak the name of a flower.
> If the consultant is a Kshatriya <note>the warrior/ruling class</note>, have them provide the name of a river. || 49 ||
> When the consultant is of the Vaishya <note>the merchant/trader class</note> caste, they should utter the name of a deity.
> A wise practitioner should have a Shudra <note>the laboring class</note> name a fruit for the purpose of the query. || 50 ||

**Syntax:** When the consultant is a Brahmin, then from their mouth a flower name; if the asker is a Kshatriya, then have them give a river name. || 49 ||
When the asker is of the Vaishya caste, they should utter a deity's name. The wise one should take the name of some fruit from the mouth of a Shudra for the query. || 50 ||

**Richa <note>Commentary</note>:** If the consultant is a Brahmin, have them say a flower's name; if a Kshatriya, a river's name; if a Vaishya, the name of a deity; and if a Shudra, the name of a fruit. Based on the numerical values assigned to the vowels and consonants appearing in those names, one should calculate the <term>Pindanka</term> <note>the "Body-Number" or sum total used for divination</note> according to the methods described later to provide the answer. || 49-50 ||

**Caution:** According to verse 48, the astrologer should accurately write down on paper the letters of the names of flowers, fruits, rivers, trees, or deities spoken by the consultant (or a child, etc.) exactly as they are pronounced. Then, the vowels and consonants should be separated and accepted. Their corresponding numbers should then be found according to the following verses and added together to create the "Pinda" (sum). For example, if the word spoken by the consultant is "Ganga," write it accurately as 'Ganga' and separate its vowels and consonants: G + a + n + g + ā—thus there will be five components. Similarly, in "Angur" (Grape): Am + g + ū + r.

**Prose Order (Anvaya):**—The fixed numbers (*dhruva*) of the letters from 'A' to 'Ha' are remembered as: Sun <gloss>12</gloss>, Nature <gloss>21</gloss>, Lord <gloss>11</gloss>, Firmness <gloss>18</gloss>, Fifteen <gloss>15</gloss>, Caste <gloss>32</gloss>, Eighteen <gloss>18</gloss>, Teeth <gloss>32</gloss>, Elements <gloss>25</gloss>, Nineteen <gloss>21</gloss> <note>The text lists 21 here, though the word means 19</note>, Element <gloss>25</gloss>, All-gods <gloss>13</gloss>, Manifestations <gloss>11</gloss>, Twenty-one <gloss>21</gloss>, Void-fire <gloss>30</gloss>, Direction <gloss>10</gloss>, Lunar day <gloss>15</gloss>, Melody <gloss>21</gloss>, Rama-eyes <gloss>23</gloss>, then Twenty-six <gloss>26</gloss>, Twenty-six <gloss>26</gloss>, Ten <gloss>10</gloss>, All-gods <gloss>13</gloss>, Eye-eyes <gloss>22</gloss>, Five-qualities <gloss>35</gloss>, Five-oceans <gloss>45</gloss>, Manu <gloss>14</gloss>, Firmness <gloss>18</gloss>, Eighteen-meter <gloss>17</gloss> <note>Usually 18, but glossed as 17</note>, Rama-moon <gloss>13</gloss>, Arrow-fires <gloss>35</gloss>, Wealth-eyes <gloss>28</gloss>, Firmness <gloss>18</gloss>, Twenty-six <gloss>18</gloss>, Stars <gloss>27</gloss>, Six-elephants <gloss>86</gloss>, Arts <gloss>16</gloss>, All-gods <gloss>13</gloss>, Three-moons <gloss>13</gloss>, Arrow-Ramas <gloss>35</gloss>, Season-eyes <gloss>26</gloss>, Arrow-Ramas <gloss>35</gloss>, Arrow-Ramas <gloss>35</gloss>, and the Suns <gloss>12</gloss>.

# First Half
Page 21

**Constant Number—** The <term>Constant Numbers (Dhruvanka)</term> <note>Fixed numerical values assigned to specific variables in this system</note> have been explained thoroughly in the previous wheel diagram.

**Additive Number—** This is also called the <term>Kshepaka</term> or <term>Kshepa</term> <note>An additive constant used to modify a sum</note>. This additive number varies depending on the specific type of question being asked. It should be taken from the context of that particular question. "Kshepakanka" is simply another name for it.

**Dividend Number—** Just as the <term>Total Sum (Pindanka)</term> is formed by adding the Constant Numbers, the <term>Dividend (Bhajyanka)</term> is formed by adding the Total Sum and the Additive Number. The answer to the question is obtained based on the remainder left after dividing this Dividend by the Divisor. The number that is divided is called the Dividend.

**Divisor Number—** This is also called the <term>Bhajaka</term>; it is the number used to divide. This also varies according to the type of question.

### || Question of Victory or Defeat ||

> In victory or defeat, the additive is declared to be thirty-four. || 57 ||
> Dividing by the "Ramas" <note>original: "Ramaih"; Rama represents the number 3 in traditional Sanskrit numerical notation</note>, if one remains, declare victory.
> If two remain, the wise should declare a stalemate; if the remainder is zero, it is defeat. || 58 ||

**Prose Construction:** For the wise, in victory or defeat, 34 is declared as the additive. Dividing by three, if the remainder is one, declare victory; if two, a stalemate; and if the remainder is zero, (declare) defeat. || 57-58 ||

**Richa Commentary—** In a question regarding victory or defeat, add 34 as the additive to the Total Sum and divide by three. If the remainder is one, there is victory; if the remainder is two, a stalemate/truce should be predicted; and a zero remainder is a sign of defeat. || 57-58 ||

### || Question of Happiness or Sorrow ||

> In happiness or sorrow, the additive is remembered by the wise as eight-and-Ramas <note>original: "ashtaramah"; 38</note>.
> Here, the division is by the "Eyes" <note>original: "lochanabhyam"; Eyes represent the number 2</note>; if one remains, declare happiness. || 59 ||
> If zero, one should know it as sorrow; such is the characteristic of happiness and sorrow.

**Prose Construction:** By the wise, in happiness and sorrow, 38 is remembered as the additive. Here, the division is by two; if one remains, declare happiness. If zero, one should know it as sorrow; (thus) the characteristic of happiness and sorrow (is to be known). || 59-59 1/2 ||

**Richa Commentary—** In a question of happiness or sorrow, add the additive 38 to the Total Sum to get the Dividend, then divide by two (the divisor). If one remains, it is happiness, and if zero remains, one should understand it as sorrow. || 60 1/2 ||

### || Question of Travel ||

> In travel, the "Rama-Ramas" <note>original: "rama ramashcha"; 33</note> are declared as the additives. || 60 ||
> Dividing by three, if one remains, travel is recorded.
> If two, staying put is indicated; if zero, it will be fruitless. || 61 ||

**Prose Order:** Regarding travel, the additive figures are declared as thirty-three <note>original: "राम रामाश्च" - Rama (3) and Rama (3), a traditional way of saying 33</note>. By dividing the total sum by three, if the remainder is one, travel is predicted. If two, a state of remaining <note>staying put</note> is indicated. If zero, it will result in failure. || 60-61 ||

**Commentary:** In questions regarding departure and travel, one should add the additive number 33 (thirty-three) to the <term>Pindanka<note>The total numerical value derived from the letters of the questioner's query</term>. Divide that total by three. If the remainder is one, the journey will take place. If the remainder is two, the journey will not occur. ## || Now, the Question of Life and Death ||

Regarding life and death, the additive figures are declared as forty.
Then, a division by three should be taken; the result is known by the remaining number. || 62 ||
With one, life should be predicted; with a remainder of two, it is achieved with great difficulty.
With zero, death is foretold; this should always be known by the wise. || 63 ||

**Prose Order:** In life and death, forty (40) additives are declared. Then a division by three is taken. The result is known by the remaining number. With one, life; and with a remainder of two, it should be called "achieved with difficulty." With zero, death is foretold. (Thus) it should always be known by the wise. || 62-63 ||

**Commentary:** In a question concerning life or death, one should add the additive number 40 (forty) to the total numerical value of the letters in the query. The sum should be divided by three. If the remainder is one, it should be said that the person will live. If the remainder is two, a condition of "achieved with difficulty" <note>meaning survival after a crisis or a very slow recovery</note> should be stated. If the remainder is zero, death will occur. || 62-63 ||

This should be applied when a question is asked regarding a sick person or a victim of an accident, or when there is doubt or a rumor regarding whether someone is alive or dead. In this way, the answer to both the current situation and the future is included in this query.

---

## || The Question of Pilgrimage ||

In a question of pilgrimage, the additive is known as thirty-nine <note>original: "नवराम" - Nine (9) and Rama (3), meaning 39</note>.
After dividing by three <note>original: "रामैर्" - by the Ramas (3)</note>, the journey occurs if the remainder is one. || 64 ||
With a remainder of two, it is known as mediocre; with a remainder of zero, there is no journey.
The fruit of the pilgrimage should thus be told by the wise from the query. || 65 ||

**Prose Order:** In a pilgrimage question, an additive of thirty-nine (39) is known. After dividing by three, if the remainder is one, the journey occurs. If the remainder is two, it is known as mediocre; if the remainder is zero, no journey. Thus, the fruit of the pilgrimage should be told by the wise from the query. || 64-65 ||

**Commentary:** After creating the sum of the fixed numbers of the pilgrimage query, one should add the additive of thirty-nine (39) to that total. Divide that sum by three. If the remainder is one, there will be an excellent journey and it will be of great significance. If the remainder is two, it will be a mediocre journey or a journey of a very short distance; however, if the remainder is zero, the journey will not happen. This is what should be told. || 64-65 ||

# First Half

-># Inquiry Regarding Rain <-

> Will there be rain, or will there be a drought?
> In the inquiry regarding rain, the wise have stated that thirty-two is the additive constant. (66)
> The intelligent person should divide [the total] by three; if the remainder is one, there will be heavy rain.
> If the remainder is two, there will be moderate rain; if the remainder is zero, there will be a drought. (67)

**Prose Order:** Whether there will be rain or drought—in this inquiry of rain, the wise have stated thirty-two as the <term>kshepaka</term> <note>an additive constant or "throw-in" number used to adjust a calculation</note>. The intelligent person should divide by three <note>original: "vahnibhir" (by the fires), a numerical code for the number 3</note>. If the remainder is one, heavy rain; if two, moderate rain; if the remainder is zero, drought. (66-67)

**Commentary:** When someone asks during the rainy season or at any other time whether it will rain or not, scholars have instructed to add thirty-two (32) to the <term>pindanka</term> <note>the base number derived from the time or letters of the question</note> for this inquiry. After dividing the sum of the base number and the additive constant by three, if the remainder is one, there will be abundant rain. If the remainder is two, the rain will be of medium intensity. If the remainder is zero, there will be no rain, meaning a drought will prevail. (66-67)

The climate of India is such that questions regarding rain can be asked at any time throughout the year. Therefore, in addition to the four months of the monsoon season, such questions are also asked regarding winter rains <note>original: "mavatha"</note> and other occasions.

-># Inquiry Regarding Pregnancy <-

> In the inquiry regarding pregnancy, the wise have stated the additive constant to be twenty-six.
> After dividing by three, if the remainder is one, it is known that there is a pregnancy. (68)
> If the remainder is two, there is doubt, or perhaps the loss of the pregnancy.
> If the remainder is zero, there is no pregnancy; this is the certainty in the inquiry of pregnancy. (69)

**Prose Order:** The wise have stated twenty-six (26) as the additive constant in the inquiry of pregnancy. After dividing by three, if the remainder is one <note>original: "bhu" (earth), a numerical code for 1</note>, a pregnancy is recorded. If the remainder is two, there is doubt or the loss of the pregnancy; if the remainder is zero, there is no pregnancy. This is the determination in the inquiry of pregnancy. (68-69)

**Commentary:** To obtain the answer to a question regarding pregnancy, one should add twenty-six (26) to the base number formed from the fixed values of the inquiry. That sum should be divided by three. If the remainder is one, then that woman is pregnant. If the remainder is two, the status of the pregnancy is doubtful; the pregnancy may be weak or might be lost. If the remainder is zero, one should state that there is no pregnancy. (68-69)

In this manner, from verses 56 to 69, the method for answering a total of eight types of questions by adding constants to the base numbers has been explained. To avoid difficulty, the <term>daivajña</term> <note>astrologer or diviner</note> should prepare a chart of these eight questions based on these verses. By looking at it, the calculation can be done immediately according to the question, and the answer can be given to the seeker. There will be no need to consult the book in front of the seeker.

He uttered "Dadima" (Pomegranate); thus, in the six letters of Dadima—D+ā+ḍ+i, m+a—the <term>Pindanka</term><note>Body-number: a numerical value derived from the sum of individual letters</note> became 17+21+22+11+86+12 = 169 (one hundred sixty-nine). When the <term>Kshepaka</term><note>Additive constant or "throw-in" number used to calibrate a specific type of question</note> of 39 for a pilgrimage question was added to this Body-number, the <term>Bhajyanka</term><note>Dividend: the number to be divided</note> became 208 (two hundred eight). Dividing this by three (208 ÷ 3), the <term>Labdhi</term><note>Quotient</note> is 69 and the remainder is 1; therefore, the pilgrimage will be of an excellent nature.

(2) If this same question had been about happiness or sorrow, then adding the additive constant of 38 to the question's Body-number 169 would result in a dividend of 207. Dividing this by two, if the remainder were 1, it would indicate a result bringing happiness.

## || Now, the Consideration of the Silent Question ||

> Divide the Body-number by three: if one remains, it is Life; if two, Metal; if zero, Root. || 70 ||

**Connection:** The meaning is straightforward. || 70 ||

**Commentary:** Based on the previously calculated Body-number, the answer to a <term>Muka Prashna</term><note>Silent Question: a divination where the seeker thinks of a question but does not speak it aloud</note> is also given. In the consideration of a silent question, one does not add an additive constant; rather, the "Body" (sum) created based on the letters of the word spoken by the seeker is considered the dividend. This is divided by three. If the remainder is 1, it should be known as a question regarding **Life** (living beings); if 2 remains, a question regarding **Metal** (minerals/inanimate objects); and if the remainder is zero, it should be known as a question regarding the **Root** (vegetation/plants). || 70 ||

**Example—** (1) Suppose that upon being asked a silent question, the <term>Daivajña</term><note>Astrologer or Diviner; literally "knower of the divine"</note> asked the seeker to name any fruit. He said "Seva" (Apple). Therefore, from the fixed-number chart, the four numbers S=35 + e=18 + v=35 + a=12 were taken. The sum of all four = 100 (one hundred). Dividing this by three (100 ÷ 3), the quotient is 33 and the remainder is 1. The answer is: "The seeker's question is related to **Life**."

(2) If the word 'Gulaba' (Rose) had come from the seeker's mouth, then from the six letters of Gulaba—G+u+l+ā + b + a—the values 21 + 15 + 13 + 21 + 26 + 12 resulted in a Body-number of 108. Dividing this by three, the remainder is zero. Therefore, according to the phrase "Zero indicates the Root," the question would be identified as relating to the **Root** (botanical).

(3) Similarly, suppose the diviner asked the seeker to name a river for the silent question consideration, and the seeker said "Yamuna." Then its letters are Y+a+m+u+n+ā. The numbers obtained from these are 16+12+86+15+35+21. Their sum is 185. This is the "Body." Dividing this by three, the remainder is 2. Then the answer to the silent question will be that it is a **Metal-related** (inorganic) question.

## || Categories of Life ||

> From the perspective of Life, living beings are of four types: Biped (two-legged), Quadruped (four-legged), Multiped (many-legged), and Footless. From the remainder of the Body-number divided by four: one indicates a Biped; two, a Quadruped; three, a Multiped; and four (zero), the Footless. || 71 || Among Bipeds, there are three types: In the 'Embraced' state, a man; in the 'Smoky' state, a woman; in the 'Burnt' state, a eunuch. || 72 ||

# 26
## Compendium of Kerala Inquiry Science
<note>original: "Kerala-Prashna-Shastra-Sangraha"</note>

**The Connection—is straightforward.** || 71-72 ||

**Richa Commentary:** When the determination of an inquiry regarding a "Living Being" <note>Jiva</note> has been established by the previously mentioned method, one should then divide that same previously calculated Total Numerical Value <term>Pindanka: a sum derived from the numerical values of the letters in a query</term> by four. 

*   If **one** remains: the concern relates to a **Biped** (two-footed).
*   If **two** remain: the inquiry is about a **Quadruped** (four-footed).
*   If **three** remain: the inquiry relates to a **Multipod** (many-footed).
*   If **four** (or zero) remain: the inquiry concerns a **Footless** being.

Within these four categories: **Bipeds** include humans, monkeys, langurs, chimpanzees, gorillas, etc. **Quadrupeds** are four-footed animals. **Multipods** include creatures like centipedes, and **Footless** beings include animals such as snakes. || 71 ||

Once a Biped has been determined, there are three further divisions: Male, Female, and Eunuch. Therefore:
*   If the querent made an **Embraced** <note>Alingita: a classification of breath or planetary state</note> inquiry: it is a **Male Biped**.
*   If it was a **Smoked** <note>Abhidhumita</note> inquiry: it is a **Female Biped**.
*   If it was a **Burnt** <note>Dagdha</note> inquiry: it is a **Eunuch** <note>Shandha</note> **Biped**. || 72 ||

**For Example:** In a "Silent Inquiry" <note>Muka Prashna: where the querent does not speak their question aloud</note>, if the word "Narmada" (the name of a river) is uttered by the querent as the inquiry-letters, the calculation is as follows:
N (35) + a (12) + r (13) + m (86) + a (12) + d (17) + a (21) = **Total 196 Pindanka**.
Dividing this 196 by three leaves a remainder of one, thus determining it is a "Living Being" inquiry. Then, dividing this same 196 by four (196 ÷ 4) results in a quotient of 49 and a **remainder of zero (or four)**. Thus, it is settled that the inquiry relates to a **Footless being** (such as a snake).

->### Classification of Metals/Minerals ###<-

Metals/Minerals are of two types: **Fusible** <note>Dhamya: those that can be melted/refined by fire</note> and **Non-fusible** <note>Adhamya</note>. Here, divide by two. With one remaining, it is Fusible; with two, it is Non-fusible. || 73 ||

**The Connection—is straightforward.** || 73 ||

**Richa Commentary:** Metals/Minerals <term>Dhatu</term> are of two types: Fusible and Non-fusible. (Those which can be melted in fire and do not abandon their essential properties even when melted are called **Fusible**). If one remains after dividing the Total Numerical Value by two, it should be called a Fusible metal; if two remain, it is a Non-fusible mineral. || 73 ||

If you divide the total number by eight and one remains, it is gold; if two remain, silver; if three remain, copper; if four remain, bronze; if five remain, brass; if six remain, tin <note>original: "vanga"</note>; if seven remain, lead; and if eight remain, it should be known as iron. These metallic substances are further divided into two types: wrought <note>original: "ghatit"</note> and unwrought <note>original: "aghatit"</note>. ->|| The Primary Divisions of Plants ||<-

**Plant life is of four types: Trees, Shrubs, Creepers, and Climbers. Dividing the total by four: if one remains, it is a Tree. If two, a Shrub. If three, a Creeper such as pumpkin or water chestnut. If four, a Climber such as grass, grain, durva grass, or wheat. These are further divided into two types: edible and inedible. Dividing the base number by two: if one remains, it is edible; if two, it is inedible. These are again divided into two: fragrant and foul-smelling. Dividing the base number by two: if one remains, it is fragrant; if two, it is foul-smelling. || 75 ||**

The connection is simple. || 75 ||

Commentary — Plant life <note>original: "mula," literally "root," used here for the plant kingdom</note> is of four types: Trees, Shrubs <note>original: "gulma"</note>, Creepers <note>original: "valli"</note>, and Climbers <note>original: "lata"</note>. Divide the question's base number by four. If one remains, it is a tree; if two remain, a shrub; if three remain, a creeper like pumpkin, water chestnut, etc. If four remain, it should be known as a climber, meaning straw, grass, wheat, sorghum, or millet. This plant category again has two divisions: edible and inedible. Dividing the base number by two, if one remains it is edible (fit for consumption), and if two remain it is inedible. Furthermore, there are two more divisions: fragrant and foul-smelling. If one remains, the plant is fragrant and tasty; if two remain, it should be understood as foul-smelling and distasteful.

Here, some scholars argue that substances like vetiver <note>original: "khas"</note> or sandalwood, though fragrant, are inedible. However, in my opinion, they are not "inedible" in a divinatory sense. An object cannot be considered inedible simply because it is not a staple food. The definition of edible and inedible also changes from the perspective of religious law <note>Dharmashastra</note>. Similarly, there will be a difference between edible and inedible from a medical science perspective. Therefore, an astrologer must decide on this matter by considering the country, time, circumstances, lineage, customs, and traditions. The answer to the question should be given with extreme caution. || 75 ||
