### || Knowledge of Lost Objects ||

**If the messenger asks a question while the astrologer <note>original: "Daivagya"</note> is looking at the sky, the lost object should be said to be in the sky (above). If looking downwards, it should be said to be below. If the person enters a corner to ask, it should be said to be in a corner. In whichever direction they enter and look, the object should be declared to be there. On whichever day they ask, the recovery of the lost item should be predicted for that same day.** || 77 ||

**Connection —** Simple. || 77 ||

**Commentary —** When the petitioner or messenger asks a question while looking toward the sky in the sight of the astrologer, then it should be said that the lost object is on the roof of the house, an upper floor, atop a tree, or on a high mound, etc. If at the time of asking, the petitioner's gaze is toward the ground, then the possibility of that object is underground, in a cellar, a well, or a step-well, etc., or it remains buried in the earth. If the seeker's gaze is in a corner or they enter from a corner (Northeast, Southeast, Southwest, or Northwest) to ask the question, then the object should be described as being in a corner. The direction in which the petitioner looks or the direction from which they enter is the direction where the object is likely to be. Similarly, the lost object will be recovered on the same day of the week <note>original: "Vaar"</note> on which the petitioner asked the question. || 77 ||

In my opinion, the recovery of the lost object will either happen on that very day, or the day of recovery will fall on that same day of the week. This duration could also span days, months, or years.

### || Knowledge of Lost Objects by Another Method ||

**Divide the fixed question number <note>original: "Dhruva-anka"</note> by twelve; the remaining numbers should be known as the zodiac signs starting from Aries. If Aries: in the village; Taurus: in the field; Gemini: at a crossroads; Cancer: in the underworld (underground); Leo: in the atmosphere (above); Virgo: in an empty house; Libra: on the path; Scorpio: in the house; Sagittarius: in the village; Capricorn: in the atmosphere; Aquarius: in a pond; Pisces: on a riverbank or in the Ganges.** || 78 ||

**Connection —** Simple. || 78 ||

**Commentary —** After dividing the "Pinda-anka" (the calculated sum of the question) created by the previously mentioned method by twelve, the remainder indicates the zodiac sign, and the prediction should be made according to that sign. For example—if one remains, it is Aries <note>original: "Mesha"</note>; the lost object is in that same village, city, or another village. If two remain, the lost object is possible in a field, open ground, or farmhouse; at that time, know it as Taurus <note>original: "Vrishabha"</note>. If three remain, it is Gemini <note>original: "Mithuna"</note>, and one should say the lost object is near a crossroads. In a remainder of four, it is Cancer <note>original: "Karka"</note>; in Cancer, the lost object is in the "Rasatala" (underground). Whereas if five remain, in Leo <note>original: "Simha"</note>, the lost object is in the atmosphere (roof or other high places). If six remain, in Virgo <note>original: "Kanya"</note>...

### || Knowledge of the Duration of Work ||

-> According to Muladeva, if the state is "Embraced" <note>Alingita</note>, the time is said to be days; if "Smoky" <note>Abhidhumita</note>, it is months; and if "Burnt" <note>Dagdha</note>, the time is said to be a year. || 80 || <-

**Prose Order:** It is spoken by Muladeva that in the *Alingita* state, days are declared; in *Abhidhumita*, months; and in *Dagdha*, a year is declared. || 80 ||

**Commentary:** According to Muladeva, if the query is "Embraced," the period for the success of the work is a few days. In a "Smoky" query, the period for success is within one or a few months. In a "Burnt" query, the work is accomplished in one year or more. || 80 ||

### || Knowledge of Work Duration by Another Method ||

-> The sum of the lunar day <note>Tithi</note>, the weekday <note>Vara</note>, and the lunar mansion <note>Nakshatra</note> should be multiplied by three and then increased by six. Divide this total by nine and determine the result from the remainder. || 81 || <-

-> With a remainder of one, the time is a fortnight; with two, months; with three, a season <note>Ritu; two months</note>; with four, a half-year <note>Ayana; six months</note>. Following these are days, then the sun <note>likely referring to a full solar cycle</note>, then a watch <note>Yama; 3 hours</note>, then Ghatis <note>24 minutes</note>, and Palas <note>24 seconds</note> as declared. || 82 ||

<note>The "Embraced, Smoky, Burnt" states refer to specific technical conditions of the breath or planetary positions used to predict how soon an event will occur.</note>

--- Page 40 ---
<meta>This page continues the instructional manual on divination and horary astrology, specifically focusing on calculating the timing of events and predicting economic conditions.</meta>

<page-num>31</page-num>
<header>First Half</header>

**Verse Construction—** The lunar date, the day of the week, the constellation, and the union <note>original: "yoga" - a specific mathematical division of the ecliptic</note> should be multiplied by three and then added to six. Divide this total by nine and determine the result from the remainder. If one remains, the period is a fortnight; if two, months; if three, a season; if four, a half-year. Following this, the results are declared as a day, a zodiac sign <note>interpreted here as a night cycle</note>, a watch, a ghadi, and a moment. ॥ 81-82 ॥

**Commentary—** At the time a question is asked, one should take the numerical values of the lunar date <term>Tithi: a lunar day</term>, the day of the week <term>Vara</term>, the star constellation <term>Nakshatra</term>, and the astrological union <term>Yoga</term> from the almanac and add them together. Multiply that sum by three. Add six to the product, then divide this total by nine. 

If the remainder is one, the work will be accomplished within one fortnight (fifteen days). If two remains, the work will be successful within one month. If three remains, success will come within one season (two months). If four remains, the work will be completed within one half-year <term>Ayana: the six-month period of the sun's transit</term>. If five remains, it will happen within one day; if six remains, within the night; if seven remains, within one watch <term>Prahar: a three-hour period</term>; if eight remains, within one <term>Ghati: a 24-minute period</term>; and if nine (or zero) remains, the work will be successful within just a few moments. This is how it should be told. ॥ 81-82 ॥

### Clarification—
(1) The calculation of the lunar date should begin from the first day of the bright fortnight. To find the number for dates after the full moon, add 15 to the date; for example, if a question is asked on the first day of the dark fortnight, one should take 1 + 15 = 16 as the date. The number for the new moon is thirty.

(2) Count the day of the week starting from Sunday. If it is Wednesday, its number will be four.

(3) The constellations are counted starting from Ashwini.

(4) The astrological unions are counted starting from Vishkambha.

### Example—
Suppose someone asks at noon on Wednesday, the sixth day of the bright fortnight of the month of Phalguna in the Vikrami Year 2058: "By when will my work be done?" Looking at the almanac for that day, it is the 6th lunar date; therefore, its number is 6. The number for Wednesday is 4. The constellation is Rohini, so its number is 4. The union is Priti, so its number is 2. Thus, the sum of Date 6 + Day 4 + Constellation 4 + Union 2 is 16. This is tripled: 16 × 3 = 48. Six is added to this: 48 + 6 = 54. This number is divided by nine: 54 ÷ 9. The quotient 6 is discarded, and the remainder is zero or nine. Therefore, one should say that the work will be successful immediately.

## || Inquiry into Victory and Defeat ||

Victory by the "Embraced" state. Peace by the "Smoky" state. Defeat by the "Burnt" state. || 84 ||

**Prose Order—** Simple. || 84 ||

**Commentary—** If the inquiry takes the form of the "Embraced" <note>original: "Alingita" - a technical term in breath-based or elemental divination</note> state, the side of the inquirer wins. If the inquiry is "Smoky" <note>original: "Abhidhumita"</note>, a peace treaty is formed between both sides. If the inquiry is "Burnt" <note>original: "Dagdha"</note>, the inquirer suffers defeat in battle. || 84 ||

## || Knowledge of Victory and Defeat by Total Numerical Value ||

When the total numerical value is divided by three: victory with one; peace with two; defeat with zero. || 85 ||

**Prose Order—** Simple.

**Commentary—** After dividing the total numerical value <term>Pindanka (the sum of values assigned to the letters of the question)</term> by three: if one remains, there is victory; if two remain, there is peace; and if three or zero remains, there is defeat in war or dispute. || 85 ||

## || Another Method for Deciding Victory or Defeat ||

If asking from the right, victory. From the left, defeat. In front, peace; from behind, death—so it was said by Muladeva. || 86 ||

**Prose Order—** Simple. || 86 ||

**Commentary—** If the inquirer asks while positioned to the right of the astrologer, there is victory. If they sit and ask from the left side, there is loss. If they ask from the front, there is peace, and an inquiry from behind the back is a harbinger of death. || 86 ||

## || Inquiry into Truth and Falsehood ||

Divide the total numerical value by two. With one, it is truth. With two, it is falsehood. || 87 ||

**Prose Order—** Simple. || 87 ||

**Commentary—** One should divide the total numerical value of the letters in the question by two. If one remains, the news heard is true. However, if two or zero remains, it should be considered false. || 87 ||

This inquiry is made when a rumor spreads or when an event cannot be confirmed.

## || Inquiry into Male or Female ||

The value of the letters of the question, the value of the vowel signs, the lunar day, the day of the week, and the star constellation are added together. By the remainder of a division by seven: if even, a woman; if odd, a man. || 88 ||

**Prose Order—** The question-letter-value, vowel-value, lunar day, weekday, and constellation are joined; by the remainder of the division by seven, even is woman, odd is man. || 88 ||

Richa—Add the date and the number of the lunar mansion <note>Nakshatra</note> to the <term>Varnanka</term> (numerical value of letters) or <term>Pindanka</term> (total numerical sum) of the letters and vowels of the query. Then divide the total by seven. If the remainder is an even number (2, 4, 6), the query should be understood as related to a woman. However, if the remainder is an odd number (1, 3, 5, 0), it should be understood as a query related to a man. || 88 ||

Example—Suppose someone asks a question on Friday, the 2nd day of the dark fortnight of the month of Phalguna in the <term>Samvat</term> <note>The traditional Hindu era, 57 years ahead of the Gregorian calendar</note> year 2058, during the Hasta lunar mansion in the morning. The child or querent speaks the word "Kamal" (Lotus). Then, according to the previously mentioned verses 51 to 55, the numerical values of the query's letters and vowels are as follows: K=13 + a=12 + m=86 + a=12 + l=13 + a=12 = Total sum = 148. To this, add the sum of the date (17), the day (6), and the lunar mansion (13), which equals 36. The grand total becomes 184. Dividing this by seven leaves a remainder of 2 (an even number); therefore, it is decided that this query relates to a woman.

## || Query Regarding Pregnancy ||

->Multiply the day of the week by three and add the lunar date.<-
->Divide by two; if the remainder is odd, there is a pregnancy; if even, there is not. || 89 ||<-

Connection: The day of the week should be multiplied by three and added to the lunar dates; when divided by two, if the remainder is odd, there is (a fetus), if even, there is not. || 89 ||

Richa—In a query regarding pregnancy, take the number of the day of the week at the time of the query (counting from Sunday) and triple it. Add the number of the lunar date <note>Tithi</note> to that product. Then divide this sum by two. If the remainder is one, say that there is a pregnancy; if the remainder is zero, say there is no pregnancy. || 89 ||

For example—Suppose the day of the query is Thursday and the lunar date is the fifth. The number for the day is 5; tripling it makes fifteen. Adding the date number 5 makes a total of 20 (twenty). Dividing by two leaves zero or two (even); therefore, one must state that there is no pregnancy. ||

## || Knowledge of the Birth of a Son or Daughter ||

->The sum of the date, day, and lunar mansion, joined with the letters of the name.<-
->Divided by seven; if the remainder is even, a girl; if odd, a son. || 90 ||<-

Connection: The sum of the date, day, and astrological combinations <note>Yogas</note> joined with the letters of the name; by the remainder of the division by seven, if even, a girl; if odd, a son. || 90 ||

Richa—Add the numbers of the lunar date, the day of the week, the lunar mansion, and the <term>Vishkambhadi Yogas</term> <note>a set of 27 specific astrological mathematical combinations</note> at the time of the query. To this sum, also add the total numerical value of the letters in the querent's name. Divide this grand total by seven; if the remainder is an even number, one should know a girl will be born. If an odd remainder is left, one should predict the birth of a son. || 90 ||

One should separately sum the <term>Dhruvankas</term> <note>fixed numerical constants assigned to specific letters</note> of the letters of the querent's name and their vowels (vowels and consonants). ||

## || Determining Son or Daughter via the Total Number ||

Divide the <term>Pindanka</term><note>The 'Pindanka' is a specific numerical value derived from the time or circumstances of the query.</note> by three. If the remainder is one, a son will be born. If the remainder is two, a daughter. If the remainder is zero, there is no pregnancy. || 91 ||

**Connection** — Simple. || 91 ||

**Commentary** — One should divide the query's total number by three. If one remains, a son will be born. If two remain, a daughter will be born. If zero remains, one should say there is no pregnancy. || 91 ||

## || Determining Son or Daughter via Body Contact ||

If the querent touches the lips, throat, neck, forehead, ears, head, or nails while asking, then a son will be born. If they touch the navel, hands, feet, or breasts while asking, then a daughter will be born. || 92 ||

**Connection** — Simple. || 92 ||

**Commentary** — If the person asking the question touches their own lips, throat, neck, forehead, ear, head, or any of the nails, it should be predicted that a son will be born. If the querent touches the navel, hand, foot, or breasts, then a daughter's birth should be predicted. || 92 ||

## || Inquiry Regarding the Paternity of the Fetus ||

When the question arises: "Is this fetus mine or another's?" Then the <term>Yoga</term><note>Yoga refers to one of the 27 astrological divisions of time in the Hindu calendar (Panchang).</note> should be multiplied by five and the day of the week should be added. || 93 ||
Divide this by three; if the remainder is one, the child is born of one's own body. If two, know it is from another. If three (zero) remains, it is born of one's own seed. || 94 ||

**Connection** — When the question is "Is this fetus mine or another's?", the number of the Yoga should be multiplied by five and the day of the week added. Divide by three; if the remainder is one, it is from one's own body; if two, from another; if three (zero) remains, know it is from one's own seed. || 93-94 ||

**Commentary** — When someone asks whether a pregnancy is theirs or someone else's, look at the <term>Panchang</term><note>The traditional five-limbed astrological calendar.</note> for the day of the query to see which of the <term>Vishkambha</term> etc. Yogas is active. Multiply that number by five, then add the number corresponding to the day of the week. Divide this total by three. If the remainder is one, the fetus belongs to the querent. If the remainder is two, it belongs to another. If the remainder is zero, the fetus is also considered one's own. || 93-94 ||

**For Example** — If at the time of the query the <term>Vriddhi Yoga</term> is active and it is Saturday: counting from Vishkambha, Vriddhi is the 11th Yoga. Therefore, 11 x 5 = 55. Adding the day number (Saturday = 7) to this, 55 + 7 = 62. Dividing this by 3 (62 ÷ 3), the remainder is 2. The answer would be that the fetus belongs to another.

## || Marriage Inquiry ||

Divide the <term>Pindanka</term> by eight. If the remainder is one, marriage occurs without effort. If two, with difficulty...

# First Half

**Marriage: With three, it does not happen. With four, the death of the bride. With five, the death of a paternal uncle or similar relative, or travel to a foreign land. With six, fear of the king. With seven, the death of both or the death of the father-in-law. With eight, the death of offspring. Thus ends the consideration of marriage. || 95 ||**

Connection: Simple. || 95 ||

Commentary: When a question regarding marriage is asked, one should divide the total numerical value of the letters in the question by eight. If the remainder is one, the marriage will be completed easily and without effort. If the remainder is two, the marriage will occur with difficulty. If three remains, the marriage will not take place. If four remains, there is a possibility of the bride's death shortly before or after the wedding. If five remains, there will be an obstacle to the marriage or marital happiness due to the death or relocation (transfer) of the bride's paternal uncle, maternal uncle, or aunt's husband. If six remains, "fear of the king" will arise—meaning fear of legal repercussions such as the <term>Sharda Act</term> <note>The Child Marriage Restraint Act of 1929</note> or the <term>Dowry Prohibition Act</term> <note>Legislation enacted in 1961 to prevent the giving or taking of dowry</note>. If seven remains, either both husband and wife will die, or the father-in-law will die. If eight remains, the marriage will occur, but the children will not survive. In this manner, one should contemplate the question of marriage. || 95 ||

### || The Question of Life and Death ||

**Double the letter-sum. Quadruple the vowel-sum. Divide that total by three. With one, life. With two, suffering. With zero, death. || 96 ||**

Connection: It is simple. || 96 ||

Commentary: If someone asks whether a particular person will live or die, then add the fixed numerical values of the vowels and consonants of the question's letters separately. The sum of the values of the consonants is called the <term>Akshara-pinda</term> <note>Letter-body or consonant-sum</note> or <term>Varna-pinda</term>. The sum of the values of the vowels is called the <term>Matra-pinda</term> <note>Measure-body or vowel-sum</note>. Multiply the letter-sum by two and the vowel-sum by four. The sum of these two products is called the <term>Samudaya</term> <note>The totality or aggregate</note>. Divide this aggregate by three. If the remainder is one, the patient is alive and will remain alive. If the remainder is two, they will have to endure pain/suffering. If the remainder is zero, death should be understood. || 96 ||

### || The Question of the Duration of Life or Death ||

**In the "Embraced," days. In the "Smoky," months. In the "Burnt," years. || 97 ||**

Connection: It is simple. || 97 ||

Commentary: If it is an <term>Alingita</term> <note>Embraced; a technical classification of the breath or planetary position in divination</note> question, the result will manifest in days. If it is an <term>Abhidhumrita</term> <note>Smoky or clouded</note> question, the result will manifest in months. However, if it is a <term>Dagdha</term> <note>Burnt or scorched</note> question, the result will only be obtained after a full year. (After determining life, suffering, or death according to verse 96, one should then provide the timing according to verse 97). || 97 ||

### || The Question of Obtaining Objects ||

**Multiply one's own letters by three, then add the sum of the letters of the object. Divide by two; if the remainder is even, there is gain; otherwise, there is not. || 98 ||**

# Compendium of Kerala Prasha Shastra
<note>original: "Kerala-Prashna-Shastra-Sangraha" — A manual on the Kerala system of horary astrology, which uses immediate questions to predict outcomes.</note>

**Prose Construction:** Multiply the letters of one's own name by three, add the letters of the object's name, and add one. Divide by two; if the remainder is even, there is gain; otherwise, say there is none. || 98 ||

**Commentary:** When the question is, "Will I obtain a certain object or not? Or will I benefit from a certain object?" then take the number of letters in the questioner's name and triple it. To this product, add the number of letters in the name of the object and add one more. Then divide this total sum by two. If the remainder is one <note>an odd number</note>, there will be no gain. If the remainder is two or zero <note>an even number</note>, there will be gain. || 98 ||

**Special Note:** Many scholars answer the question by taking the <term>Pinda</term><note>a calculated numerical value or "body" of a word</note> formed from the <term>Dhruvankas</term><note>fixed numerical values assigned to specific letters</note> of the questioner's name, tripling it, adding the numerical value of the desired object's name, adding one, and then dividing by two.

-> || Question: Will money be obtained from a specific person or not? || <-

> Multiply the master's name by three, add one's own letters, and divide by
> three. Know that gain comes with a remainder of one; with two, it does not. || 99 ||
> With a remainder of three, the money will be obtained after a long time.
> This is the method for the question: "Will money be obtained from so-and-so or not?" || 100 ||

**Prose Construction:** Multiply the master's name by three. Mix in <insert>add</insert> one's own letters. Divide by three. With a remainder of one, know there is attainment; with two, there is not. With a remainder of three, the money will be obtained after a long time. This is the method for the question of whether money will be obtained from a specific person. || 99-100 ||

**Commentary:** Regarding the person from whom money or other benefits are to be received, take the number of letters in that "master" or giver's name and multiply by three. Add the number of "one's own letters" (the number of letters in the questioner's own name) to that product. Divide this sum by three. If the remainder is one, there will be gain from that person. If the remainder is two, there will be no gain. If the remainder is three <note>or zero, in modulo 3 arithmetic</note>, the object will be obtained after many days. The decision regarding the question "Will I receive money from a specific person?" should be made using this method. || 99-100 ||

**Special Note:** When a person asks a question regarding the payment of their withheld salary, allowance, remuneration, honorarium, or bills, the answer should be given using this specific method.

<summary>This page outlines numerological formulas for predicting if a person will acquire a specific object or receive money from a particular individual, including a method for estimating the quantity of wealth gained.</summary>
<keywords>horary astrology, Kerala system, numerology, divination, wealth prediction, name calculation, Prasha Shastra</keywords>

--- Page 46 ---
<meta>This page continues the discussion of divination techniques, specifically focusing on calculating the quantity of wealth one might receive and determining the status of a messenger's journey.</meta>

Divide the remainder; these are known as "tens." The quotient should be multiplied by five and then subtracted from those "tens." According to the **status of the family** <note>original: "kula-mana"</note>, one should recognize that as the amount to be obtained. || 101-102 ||

**Commentary** — When a petitioner asks— "How much wealth (quantity) will I receive from such-and-such a person?" —to answer that, multiply the number of letters in the petitioner's name by nine. Add five to that product. Then divide this sum by seven. The remaining number should be understood as "tens." While dividing by seven, take the quotient obtained and multiply it by five, then subtract it from the previously obtained "tens." Whatever remains will be the amount of wealth received, according to the status of their family/rank. || 101-102 ||

### Clarification—

(1) The original verse uses the word *tannama*, which means— the name of the petitioner.

(2) **Family Status (Kula-mana)**— This term refers to social and official standing. In today's context, if that person is in government service, there are departments such as Gazetted Class I, Class II, Class III, and Class IV; these should be understood as the "family status." Similarly, an employee of a private sector establishment, company, or industry also holds different grades, which should also be understood as "family status."

For example, if the petitioner's name is Devesh Kumar, then D+e+v+e+sh+a+k+u+m+aa+r+a = a total of 12 letters (vowels and consonants). Multiplying these by nine: 12 x 9 = 108. Adding five to this: 108 + 5 = 113. Dividing this by seven: 113 ÷ 7 = quotient 16 — the remainder is zero or seven <note>Mathematically, 113 divided by 7 leaves a remainder of 1; the text follows a specific traditional calculation logic or contains a scribal error</note>. Thus, these are seven "tens." Multiplying the quotient 16 by five results in 16 x 5 = 75 <note>Mathematically 80; the text records 75</note>. Now, suppose the asker is a Class III employee; then seven "tens" (or hundreds or thousands) becomes seven thousand. Subtracting 75 from these seven thousand, he will receive 6925 rupees or 625 rupees—one should answer after performing such deliberation with one's intellect.

However many "tens" are obtained, one should imagine them as hundreds, thousands, ten thousands (<term>Ayuta</term> <note>A classical unit representing 10,000</note>), or lakhs <note>100,000</note> according to the status of the petitioner. In this way, answering such a question requires the purity of heart and the discernment of the <term>Astrologer</term> <note>original: "Daivajña" - one who knows the divine/fate</note>, by which the exact estimation of the proportion of wealth can be made. This method should be applied according to the country, time, and circumstances. Whether the petitioner will receive this in Rupees, Dollars, Pounds, Euros, Dinars, Lira, Yen, Yuan, or Rubles—the currency of the country—must also be carefully considered.

# Kerala Compendium of Inquiry Science
<note>original: "Kerala-Prashna-Shastra-Sangraha"</note>

**The Verse:** When the question arises whether a messenger has departed or not, the lunar date should be tripled, five added, and the day of the week mixed in. Then, multiply by seven and divide by two to state the result. If one remains, the messenger has departed; if the remainder is zero, he is stationary. || 103-104 ||

**Explanation:** When someone asks whether a messenger or a specific person has left their location or not, take the lunar date <term>Tithi; the lunar day from the Hindu calendar</term> of the time of the inquiry and triple it. Add five to that total. To this sum, add the numerical value of the day of the week <note>Sunday=1, Monday=2, etc.</note>. Multiply this entire sum by seven. Then, divide this number by two. If the remainder is one, the messenger has set out. If the remainder is zero or two <note>mathematically, a division by two only leaves 0 or 1; the text implies an even division</note>, then he has not moved. || 103-104 ||

**For Example:** Suppose someone asks about a messenger's arrival when the lunar date is the 8th (Ashtami) and the day is Tuesday (the 3rd day).
Calculation: Date 8 × 3 = 24.
Add five: 24 + 5 = 29.
Add the day's number (3): 29 + 3 = 32.
Multiply by seven: 32 × 7 = 224.
Divide by two: 224 ÷ 2 = quotient of 112 with a **remainder of 0 (zero)**.
According to the rule "if the remainder is zero, he is stationary," one must conclude the messenger has not yet departed.

### || Inquiry Regarding a Traveler ||

> The lunar date, the day, the rising sign, and the letters of the name combined;
> The lunar mansion and the half-date as well—divide the total by seven. || 105 ||
> With one, he stays there; with two, he is traveling.
> With three, he is halfway; with four, he is near the village. || 106 ||
> With five, he has turned back; with six, he is afflicted by illness.
> With seven, his work is in vain; thus the wise have explained the inquiry. || 107 ||

**Explanation:** When a person goes to a foreign land or another region for travel, they are called a traveler <term>Pantha; also known as a pilgrim or wayfarer</term>. When an inquiry is made about them, one should combine the following values from the moment of the question: the lunar date, the day of the week, the lunar mansion <term>Nakshatra</term>, the half-date <term>Karana</term>, the rising zodiac sign <term>Lagna</term>, and the number of letters in the traveler's name. Divide the sum of all these by seven.

The results are interpreted as follows:
*   **Remainder 1:** The traveler is still at the destination.
*   **Remainder 2:** He has started his journey back.
*   **Remainder 3:** He is currently in the middle of the path.
*   **Remainder 4:** He has arrived near the outskirts of the village.
*   **Remainder 5:** He started for home but turned back again from the road.
*   **Remainder 6:** He has fallen ill.
*   **Remainder 7 (or 0):** His work has not been successful; he did not find success in his employment or trade.

This is how the reading should be given. || 105-107 ||

Example—On the date February 27, 2002 (Vikram Samvat 2058, Shaka Era 1924), during the waning moon of the month of Phalguna <note>February-March</note> on the 12th day (Thursday) at 11:30 AM, a question was asked regarding a traveler named Devadatta. The ascendant was Taurus, the lunar mansion was Uttarashadha, and the Karana <note>astrological half-day</note> was Kaulava. The answer is determined as follows: In the name Devadatta (D+e+v+a+d+a+t+t+a), there are a total of nine letters (vowels + consonants). Therefore: Krishna Dwadashi Tithi (12th day) = 27 <note>The numerical value assigned to the lunar date in this system</note> + Weekday (5) + Lunar Mansion (21) + Karana (3) + Ascendant (2) + Name letters (9) = 67 (Sixty-seven). Dividing this by seven (67 ÷ 7), the remainder is 4. According to the rule "In the fourth, near the village," one should predict that the traveler has returned and is near the village or city.

### || Question Regarding Meetings ||

> Multiply the Ghatikas <note>units of time equal to 24 minutes</note> by three, add one, then add seven.
> Divide the total by four; the remainder indicates the result. || 108 ||
> If one remains, a meeting occurs; if two, a second journey is required.
> If three remain, there is no meeting; if four (zero), it causes great distress. || 109 ||

**Prose Construction:** Add one to the (desired) Ghatikas, multiply by three, then add seven and divide by four (the Vedas). The remainder indicates the result. If one remains, a meeting; if two, a meeting after another journey; if three, no meeting; if four (zero), it will be distressful. || 108-109 ||

**Commentary:** Add one to the number of Ghatikas elapsed from sunrise at the time of the question (ignore the Palas <note>units of time equal to 24 seconds</note>). Multiply them by three and then add seven. Divide this sum by four. If the remainder is one, the person you wish to meet will be met. If the remainder is two, then "at the interval of a journey," meaning you will have to go to them a second time, and then the meeting will occur. If three remain, you will not be able to meet that person. If four (zero) remain, it should be said that a meeting is possible only after much distress and hard work. || 108-109 ||

Often people have to meet ministers, officials, or distinguished individuals. Sometimes there is a need to meet relatives or other persons related to business and commerce. In such cases, this type of question is asked.

Example—Suppose someone asks, "I want to go meet a certain person; will he be met?" If the calculated Ishtakala <note>time elapsed since sunrise</note> based on local sunrise is 15 Ghatis and 35 Palas. Thus: 15 + 1 = 16; 16 x 3 = 48. Adding seven to this: 48 + 7 = 55. Dividing this by four, the remainder is three. Therefore, according to "If three remain, no meeting," the meeting with the desired person will not take place.

### || Knowledge of the Desired Person's Activities ||

> The sum of the Lunar Day, Weekday, and Lunar Mansion, multiplied by two and increased by three.
> Then divide by twelve; the remainder indicates the result. || 110 ||

**Prose Construction:** The sum of the Lunar Day (Tithi), Weekday (Vara), and Lunar Mansion (Riksha) is doubled. (And) three is added. Then divide by twelve; the remainder indicates the result. || 110 ||

**Richa <note>Commentary by the translator/editor</note>—** This inquiry is used to determine the immediate activities of a desired person, friend, relative, loved one, enemy, or even a thief. ->|| Results for Remainders of Four and Five ||<-

->If the remainder is the Vedas (four), he is asleep and cleansing his mouth with water.<-
->If the remainder is five, he was asleep but has woken up and is eating. || 114 ||<-

**Prose Order:** If the remainder is four <note>Vedas: a numerical symbol for 4</note>, he is asleep and cleansing his mouth with water. If the remainder is five, having been asleep, he has risen and is at his meal. || 114 ||

**Commentary:** According to the previously mentioned method, if a remainder of four is left, one should say that the desired person has just woken up from sleep and has washed his hands and face with water.

If five remains, one should say that the person will eat after waking up from sleep. || 114 ||

->|| Results for Remainders of Six and Seven ||<-

->If the remainder is the Flavors (six), a meeting will certainly occur mid-way on the path.<-
->If the remainder is seven, one should indicate the enjoyment of a woman's company. || 115 ||<-

**Prose Order:** If the remainder is the Flavors <note>Rasa: a numerical symbol for 6</note>, a meeting will certainly occur in the middle of the path. If the remainder is seven, one should indicate the behavior of enjoying a woman. || 115 ||

**Commentary:** Following the aforementioned process, if six remains, one should state that the desired person whom one is going to meet will definitely be encountered on the way.

If seven remains, he will be engaged in pleasure with a woman. This woman could be a wife or a lover. This is what should be stated. || 115 ||

->|| Results for Remainders of Eight and Nine ||<-

->When the remainder is eight, there will be agitation in his mind.<-
->When the remainder is the Nanda (nine), he is seen intent on religious deeds. || 116 ||<-

**Prose Order:** When the remainder is eight, then there is agitation in his heart. When the remainder is the Nanda <note>Nanda: a numerical symbol for 9</note>, he is seen intent on religious duties. || 116 ||

**Commentary:** If the number eight remains, the person one is meeting will have distress or anxiety in his mind.

If nine remains, the desired person will be found engaged in religious work, social service, or philanthropic activities. || 116 ||

<summary>This page details the specific predictions associated with remainders four through nine in a divination system, describing the target person's current activities such as sleeping, eating, traveling, or performing religious duties.</summary>

**Prose Order (Sanskrit):** If the remainder is ten, there is royal honor; if eleven, a meal; if twelve, he is sorrowful but desires the enjoyment of a woman. || 117 ||

**Commentary:** If, according to the previously mentioned method, a remainder of **ten** is left, the person inquired about is found to be receiving royal honors, awards, or a promotion.
If **eleven** remains, the desired person is found to be eating a meal.
If **twelve** remains, that person will be suffering from mental stress and will be filled with a longing for the company of a woman. || 117 ||

### || On Lost Horoscopes ||

->The constants of the letters are doubled; the constants of the vowel-marks are as before.<-
->The sum is this consideration of time, as spoken by Muladeva. || 118 ||<-

**Prose Order (Sanskrit):** The constants of the vowel-marks are as before; the constants of the letters are doubled. This sum, the consideration of time, was spoken by Muladeva. || 118 ||

**Commentary:** Based on the constants <note>Dhruva: fixed numerical values assigned to letters</note> for vowel-marks mentioned previously in verses 51 to 55, one should create a sum of the constants for the vowels in the letters of the query and leave it as it is. Then, one should double the sum of the constants for the consonants (letters) and add the two together. This method for considering a <term>Nashta-Jataka</term> <note>Lost Horoscope: the art of reconstructing a birth chart when the birth details are unknown</note> was taught by the sage Muladeva. || 118 ||

In this verse, the sum of the vowel-mark constants and the doubled letter constants forms the collective constant or <unclear>...</unclear>

<meta>This page continues the technical instructions for "Lost Horoscopy," a method used to reconstruct a birth chart when the exact time of birth is unknown. It follows the calculation of the "Pinda" (the numerical sum derived from the name) established on the previous page.</meta>

<page-num>43</page-num>
<header>First Half</header>
<vocab>Total constant sum, Year, Fortnight, Lunar mansion, Ashwini, Bharani, Krittika, Solar degrees elapsed, Month, Phalguna, Chaitra, Vaishakha, Jyeshtha, Ashadha</vocab>

This shall be called the "Total Sum of the Body" <note>Sanskrit: Pindanka Samudaya; refers to the final numerical value calculated from the letters of a person's name</note>. This is the method for creating a birth chart for someone who does not know their time of birth.

### || Determining the Year and Fortnight ||

> **Divide the total constant sum by one hundred and eight; the remainder indicates the year. Divide that same sum of letters by two; a remainder of one indicates the bright fortnight, and a remainder of two indicates the dark fortnight. || 119 ||**

**Connection: —** The meaning is straightforward. || 119 ||

**Commentary: —** According to the sum created in the previous verse 118, by dividing that total by 108, the remainder will provide the number of years passed. This reveals how many years old the subject is.

Furthermore, by dividing that same sum by two, one gains knowledge of the "Paksha" <term>Paksha: one of the two fortnights of the lunar month</term>. If the remainder is one, the birth occurred during the Waxing Moon (Shukla Paksha); if the remainder is zero or two, the birth occurred during the Waning Moon (Krishna Paksha). || 119 ||

### || Determining the Lunar Mansion ||

> **Divide that same sum of letters by twenty-seven; if one remains, it is Ashwini; if two remain, Bharani; if three remain, Krittika, and so on—thus the lunar mansions should be known. || 120 ||**

**Connection: —** The meaning is straightforward. || 120 ||

**Commentary: —** Divide the previously mentioned sum (the letter-body) by 27 <note>The original text says 24 in the commentary, likely a scribal error for 27, as there are 27 Nakshatras</note>; the birth Nakshatra <term>Nakshatra: a lunar mansion or sector of the sky</term> should be known according to the remainder. For example: a remainder of one is Ashwini, two is Bharani, three is Krittika, four is Rohini, five is Mrigashira, and so on. || 120 ||

### || Determining the Elapsed Degrees of the Sun ||

> **Divide that same sum of letters by thirty; the remainder represents the degrees. || 121 ||**

**Connection: —** Simple. || 121 ||

**Commentary: —** By dividing the aforementioned total sum by thirty, the degrees of the Sun elapsed at the time of birth will be known. <note>Each zodiac sign consists of 30 degrees; this calculation places the Sun's specific position.</note> || 121 ||

### || Determining the Month ||

> **Divide that same sum by twelve; if one remains, it is Phalguna.**
> **If two, Chaitra. If three, Vaishakha. If four, Jyeshtha.**
> **If five, Ashadha; and thus Shravana and the others should be understood. || 122 ||**

**Connection: —** The meaning is straightforward. || 122 ||

**Commentary: —** Divide the aforementioned sum by twelve. If the remainder is one, the month is Phalguna; if two, Chaitra; if three, Vaishakha; if four, Jyeshtha; if five, Ashadha; if six, Shravana; if seven, Bhadrapada, and so on. The implication is that for this specific calculation, the counting of months must begin with Phalguna <note>Approximately February/March; usually the Hindu year begins with Chaitra, but this system uses a different starting point</note>. || 122 ||

<summary>This page details the mathematical formulas used to derive a person's birth year, lunar fortnight, Nakshatra, solar degree, and birth month using only the numerical value of their name. These techniques are part of "Nashta-Jataka" or lost horoscopy for individuals lacking birth records.</summary>
<keywords>Nashta-Jataka, numerology, Nakshatra, lunar mansions, Hindu calendar months, astrological calculation, Pinda</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the technical instructions for reconstructing a lost birth chart (Nashta Jataka) based on the numerical values of spoken words, transitioning into a new section on the success or failure of inquiries.</meta>

<page-num>44</page-num>
<header>Compendium of Kerala Prashna Shastra <note>A treatise on the Kerala system of Horary Astrology</note></header>

### || Knowledge of the Ascendant at Birth ||

> In the same manner, by dividing the total numerical value of the letters <note>Akshara-pinda</note> by twelve: if one remains, it is Aries; if two, Taurus; if three, Gemini; and so on, the Ascendant <term>Lagna: the zodiac sign rising on the eastern horizon at the moment of birth</term> should be known. Thus is the method for reconstructing a lost birth chart revealed. || 123 ||

**Connection** — The meaning is straightforward. || 123 ||

**Commentary** — One should divide the previously mentioned sum of the letter-values by 12. If the remainder is 1, it is Aries; if 2, Taurus; if 3, Gemini; the sequence should be understood in this order. An astrologer should reconstruct a lost birth chart in this very manner. || 123 ||

#### Example for the Reconstruction of a Lost Birth Chart —

Suppose a Brahmin petitioner asks a question regarding a lost birth record and utters the word 'Chameli' <note>A type of Jasmine flower</note>. Upon separating the letters and vowel-marks of 'Chameli', the following fixed numerical values <term>Dhruvanka: specific numbers assigned to Sanskrit phonemes</term> are obtained:

| Letter Values | Vowel Values |
|----------|----------|
| Ch (च्) = 15 | a (अ) = 12 |
| m (म्) = 86 | e (ए) = 18 |
| l (ल्) = 13 | ī (ई) = 18 |
| —————— | —————— |
| Total = 114 x 2 | Total = 48 |
| 228 | As before = 48 |

Now, doubling the letter-sum and adding the vowel-sum (228 + 48 = 276), we get the collective total <note>Samudaya-pinda</note>. To determine the age, we divide this by 108; the remainder is 60, therefore the petitioner is sixty years old. Now, dividing 276 by 2, the remainder is zero (treated as 2), indicating a birth during the dark fortnight <note>Krishna Paksha</note>. Then, dividing 276 by 27, the remainder is 6; since the sixth constellation is Ardra, the birth star is Ardra. Dividing 276 by thirty leaves a remainder of 6, meaning the Sun had progressed 6 degrees at the time of birth. Further, dividing 276 by 12 leaves a remainder of zero (treated as 12). Counting from the month of Phalguna, the birth is established in the month of Magha; this month calculation should follow the lunar calendar starting from the bright fortnight. Again, dividing 276 by 12 leaves a remainder of zero or 12; therefore, the Ascendant at birth was Pisces <note>Meena Lagna</note>.

Based on this calculation, one should consult an almanac <term>Panchang: a traditional Hindu astrological calendar</term> from 60 years prior to the time of the inquiry, create a chart for the Pisces Ascendant, and establish the planetary positions. This chart will be accurate for making predictions. However, using this method merely for testing the astrologer's skill is not appropriate.

### || Inquiry into Success or Failure ||

> Double the number of letters in the question, add the number corresponding to the day of the week, multiply that sum by five, and divide the resulting total by seven. || 124 ||

<vocab>Birth Ascendant, Lost Nativity, Letter-sum, Vowel-sum, Fixed Number, Lunar Mansion, Ardra, Almanac, Pisces Ascendant, Inquiry of Success</vocab>

<header>First Half</header>

If one remains as a remainder, there is delay; with two or four, the work is accomplished.
With five or six, it happens instantly; but with seven, the work is destroyed. || 125 ||

**Prose Order:**—Double the number of letters in the question, then add the number of the day of the week. Multiply that sum by five; divide that total <term>Pinda<note>In Sanskrit mathematics and astrology, a 'Pinda' refers to a processed sum or a 'body' of numbers used for further calculation.</note></term> by seven (the sages).

If one or three remain, there is delay. With two or four, the work is accomplished. With five or six, it is instant; with seven, the work is destroyed. || 124-125 ||

**Richa Commentary:**—Count the number of letters in the question spoken by the inquirer, or the letters in the name of a flower offered. Double this number. Add the numerical value of the day of the week on which the question is asked <note>Sunday=1, Monday=2, etc.</note>. Multiply this sum by five; this will be your "Pinda." Divide this Pinda by seven. If the remainder is one or three, there will be a delay in the work. If the remainder is two or four, the work will be successful. If the remainder is five or six, the work will be accomplished immediately. However, if the remainder is zero or seven, the work will never be successful. || 124-125 ||

# The Compendium of Kerala Horary Astrology
## Second Half (Chapter on the Inquiry of Income/Aya)

### || Now, the Inquiries into the Banner and Other Dispositions ||

> I shall describe in this compendium various types of inquiries concerning the past and other matters. I will now present the highly remarkable chapter known as the Inquiry of Income. || 1 ||

**Prose Order—** In this compendium, I shall describe various inquiries regarding the past and others, and the most remarkable chapter named the Inquiry of Income. || 1 ||

**Commentary—** The compiler states: In this second half of the work, I describe the chapter titled "Inquiry of Income," which provides remarkable results and answers related to the past, future, and present. || 1 ||

### || The Method of Knowing the Income ||

> The eight dispositions, beginning with the Banner, are determined by the sequence of letters in the name of the fruit uttered by the seeker. Alternatively, the astrologer should always calculate them from the initial letters of the inquiry itself. || 2 ||

**Prose Order—** The eight incomes, Banner and the rest, are derived from the alphabetical order of the name of the fruit uttered; or they should always be calculated by the astrologer from the letters of the inquiry. || 2 ||

**Commentary—** Based on the first letter of the name of a fruit, flower, tree, river, or deity uttered by the seeker—or by the first letter of the inquiry itself—the <term>Daivajña</term> <note>an astrologer or "knower of fate"</note> should determine the eight <term>Ayas</term> <note>dispositions or mathematical remainders used to determine the quality of an outcome</note> such as the Banner and others. || 2 ||

### || Further Clarification of the Meaning ||

> Having understood the results through the initial letters of the uttered fruits and the rest, one should know the groups starting with the letter 'A' as the Banner and others in sequence. || 3 ||

**Prose Order—** By the power of the first letter of the uttered fruits and others, and knowing the groups of letters starting with 'A' as the Banner and the rest in order, one should declare the result. || 3 ||

**Commentary—** In this verse, the author speaks with greater clarity: based on the first letter of the question or the fruit named by the seeker, one should identify the eight groups of letters—'A', 'Ka', 'Ca', 'Ta', 'Ta', 'Pa', 'Ya', and 'Sha'—as the eight incomes (Banner, etc.) in their respective order. Thereafter, one should provide the result of the inquiry according to the methods described in this scripture. || 3 ||

### || Now, the Eight Names ||

> The Banner, the Smoke, the Lion, the Dog, the Bull, the Donkey, the Elephant, and the Crow: these are known as the eight incomes, yielding auspicious and inauspicious results in sequence. || 4 ||

<summary>This page introduces the "Aya" system of Kerala astrology, explaining how to determine eight symbolic dispositions (like the Banner or Lion) based on the first letter of a seeker's question to predict the future.</summary>
<keywords>Kerala Prashna Shastra, Horary Astrology, Aya, Banner disposition, divination methods, Sanskrit astrology, Daivajña</keywords>

# Second Half
Page 47

**Prose Order:** The <term>Flag</term> (Dhvaja), the <term>Smoke</term> (Dhumra), the <term>Lion</term> (Simha), the <term>Dog</term> (Shvana), the <term>Bull</term> (Vrisha), the <term>Donkey</term> (Khara), the <term>Elephant</term> (Gaja), and the <term>Crow</term> (Dhvanksha) are the eight <term>Income-types</term> (Aya). Their results should be known as auspicious and inauspicious respectively. || 4 ||

**Verse Commentary:** (1) Flag, (2) Smoke, (3) Lion, (4) Dog, (5) Bull, (6) Donkey, (7) Elephant, and (8) Crow are the eight <term>Income-types</term> <note>original: "Aya" - a technical term in Vedic architecture and divination referring to directional or numerical influences</note>. Their results occur in order as auspicious and inauspicious. For example—Flag is auspicious, then Smoke is inauspicious, then Lion is auspicious, and so on. || 4 ||

**Clarification:** If the first letter of a fruit or a question belongs to the 'A' group <note>vowels</note>, the Income-type will be the Flag. If the first letter is from the 'Ka' group, the Income-type will be Smoke. Suppose someone utters "Amra" (Mango) to ask a question; since its first letter belongs to the 'A' group, the Income-type for this question is the Flag. If someone utters "Bakula" (a flower), the 'Ba'—which is the first letter—falls under the 'Sha' group (the eighth group), therefore the Income-type will be the Crow. "Ganga" (River) belongs to the 'Ka' group and its Income-type is Smoke. "Pankaja" (Lotus), because its initial letter 'Pa' falls in the 'Pa' group, its type is the Donkey. The first letter of "Rajanigandha" (Tuberose) is within the 'Ya' group, so its Income-type will be the Elephant. "Chameli" (Jasmine) belongs to the 'Cha' group and is a flower falling under the Lion Income-type. In this manner, one should consider the Income-types.

->|| The Lords of the Flag and Others ||<-

> In the Flag, the Sun is to be known, and in Smoke, Mars likewise.
> In the Lion, Venus is to be known, and in the Dog, Mercury likewise. || 5 ||
> In the Bull, Jupiter is to be known, and in the Donkey, the Son of the Sun (Saturn).
> In the Elephant and the Crow, the Moon and Rahu are remembered as the lords. || 6 ||

**Prose Order:** The Sun is in the Flag, and Mars is to be known in Smoke. Venus is in the Lion, and Mercury is to be known in the Dog. Jupiter is in the Bull, and the Son of the Sun (Saturn) is to be known in the Donkey. The Moon is in the Elephant and Rahu is in the Crow; these are remembered as the lords. || 5-6 ||

<header>Compendium of Kerala Prashna Science</header>

**Richa Commentary —** The Sun is the lord of the Banner (Dhvaja) Aya; Mars of the Smoke (Dhumra); Venus of the Lion (Simha); Mercury of the Dog (Shvana); Jupiter of the Bull (Vrisha); Saturn of the Donkey (Khara); the Moon of the Elephant (Gaja); and Rahu <note>The North Node of the Moon</note> is the lord of the Crow (Dhvanksha) Aya. || 5-6 ||

### Clarification of the First Letter of the Question —
(1) There is no distinction made between short and long vowels.
(2) The letter ‘Ksha’ is a combination of ‘K + Sha’ and therefore falls under the K-group (Kavarga).
(3) The letter ‘Tra’ is ‘T + Ra’ and therefore falls under the T-group (Tavarga).
(4) The letter ‘Gya’ is ‘J + Nya’ and is included within the Ch-group (Chavarga).
(5) The vowel ‘Ri’ is included within the A-group (Avarga).

->|| General Results of the Banner and Other Ayas in Inquiry ||<-

> In the Banner, Elephant, Lion, and Bull, success is certain.
> In the Crow, Dog, Donkey, and Smoke, success in the task will not occur. || 7 ||

**Prose Order:** Success in the task certainly occurs in the Banner, Elephant, Lion, and Bull Ayas. Success does not occur in the Crow, Dog, Donkey, and Smoke Ayas. || 7 ||

**Richa Commentary —** If an inquiry is made during the four Ayas of Banner, Elephant, Lion, and Bull, the task is accomplished quickly. In the four Ayas of Crow, Dog, Donkey, and Smoke, the task is not successful. || 7 ||

**Special Note —** It is a unique feature of Sanskrit, the language of the gods, that the essence of a word is clearly revealed alongside the word itself. Here, the qualities of the eight Ayas are manifested exactly according to their names:

(1) **Banner (Dhvaja) —** Banner means a flag or standard. It is a symbol of victory, fame, glory, honor, and authority. Therefore, in a question falling under the Banner Aya, success is achieved; the work is accomplished.

(2) **Smoke (Dhumra) —** This means smoke. Smoke is a symbol of darkness and lack of clarity. It causes distress to the eyes. Therefore, at the time of inquiry, the Smoke Aya results in failure, lack of accomplishment, confusion, and doubt.

(3) **Lion (Simha) —** This is a symbol of bravery, valor, physical strength, and self-respect. Thus, when the Lion Aya occurs at the time of a question, the task is accomplished through the physical strength and effort of the querent.

(4) **Dog (Shvana) —** The dog is a creature that wags its tail merely to fill its stomach and changes its loyalty for scraps. If the Dog Aya is present at the time of inquiry, the task is ruined due to betrayal by a trusted person.

(5) **Bull (Vrisha) —** A symbol of contentment, courage, and strength, the Bull Aya represents a philanthropic nature. At the time of inquiry, this Aya brings success through the help of others.

(6) **Donkey (Khara) —** This animal is a symbol of foolishness. If the Donkey Aya occurs at the time of inquiry, a nearly completed task is spoiled due to the querent's carelessness, intoxication, or error. In this, loss occurs due to the faults in the querent's own speech.

<meta>This page continues the explanation of the eight "Ayas" (income/directional archetypes) used in divination and architectural astrology, specifically focusing on the final two types and their application to specific queries regarding existence, profit, and lost items.</meta>

<page-num>49</page-num>
<header>Second Half</header>

(7) <term>Gaja (Elephant)</term>—This creature, who walks in its own joyful state without worrying about others and provides for its own family, is extremely auspicious. It is also a symbol of Lord Ganesha. Therefore, there is guaranteed success in this *Aya* <note>Aya refers to a mathematical remainder used in Vedic astrology to determine the character of a space or undertaking</note>.

(8) <term>Dhvanksha (Crow)</term>—This refers to the creature known as the crow. It is a symbol of cunning and selfishness. If the *Dhvanksha Aya* occurs at the time of a query, the querent's work is ruined through fraud and injustice committed by wicked people.

### || The Question of Existence or Non-Existence ||

> In the Flag, Elephant, Lion, and Bull, existence is certain.
> In the Crow, Dog, Donkey, and Smoke, non-existence is declared. || 8 ||
<note>original: "ध्वज कुञ्जर सिंहेषु, वृषे चास्ति विनिश्चितम्। ध्वाङ्क्षे श्वाने खरे धूत्रे नास्तीति समुदाहृतम् ॥"</note>

<term>Prose Order</term>—In the Flag, Elephant, Lion, and Bull, existence is certain. In the Crow, Dog, Donkey, and Smoke, it is declared as non-existence. || 8 ||

<term>Richa (Commentary)</term>—Flag, Elephant, Lion, and Bull—these *Ayas* are indicators of "Is" (existence); and Crow, Dog, Donkey, and Smoke—these are indicators of "Is Not" (non-existence). || 8 ||

<term>Explanation</term>—When a question is asked: "Is such-and-such person, object, creature, or wealth present at such-and-such place or not?" then the answer should be given as Yes or No according to this verse. For example, if someone asks whether wealth is buried in a certain spot or not, this method should be used for the answer.

### || The Question of Profit and Loss ||

> In the Flag, Elephant, Bull, and Lion, a quick profit will surely occur.
> In the Crow, Dog, Donkey, and Smoke, there is destruction and strife. || 9 ||
<note>original: "ध्वजे, गजे, वृषे सिंहे शीघ्रलाभो भवेद् ध्रुवम्। ध्वाङ्क्षे श्वाने खरे धूत्रे नाशश्च कलहप्रदः ॥"</note>

<term>Prose Order</term>—In the Flag, Elephant, Bull, and Lion, a quick profit will surely occur. In the Crow, Dog, Donkey, and Smoke, there is destruction and the bringing of strife. || 9 ||

<term>Richa (Commentary)</term>—In these four *Ayas*—Flag, Elephant, Bull, and Lion—there is a guaranteed profit in questions regarding gain or loss. However, if the Crow, Dog, Donkey, or Smoke *Ayas* are present at the time of the query, there is destruction of the work and quarreling. || 9 ||

### || The Question of Gaining or Losing Lost Items ||

> In the Flag, Elephant, Bull, and Lion, the recovery of the lost item is certain.
> In the Crow, Smoke, Donkey, and Dog, loss occurs definitely. || 10 ||
<note>original: "ध्वजे, गजे, वृषे, सिंहे, नष्टलाभो भवेद् ध्रुवम्। ध्वाङ्क्षे, धूम्रे, खरे, श्वाने हानिर्भवति निश्चितम् ॥"</note>

<term>Prose Order</term>—In the Flag, Elephant, Bull, and Lion, the recovery of the lost (object) will surely occur. In the Crow, Smoke, Donkey, and Dog, loss will definitely happen. || 10 ||

<term>Richa (Commentary)</term>—In these *Ayas*—Flag, Elephant, Bull, and Lion—if the question concerns a lost object, it is definitely found. When the Crow, Smoke, Donkey, or Dog *Ayas* are present at the time of the query, the lost item is not obtained; it is not returned. || 10 ||

<summary>This page concludes the descriptions of the Elephant and Crow archetypes and provides specific mnemonic verses for divining whether an object exists, whether a venture will be profitable, or if a lost item will be recovered.</summary>

## || Inquiry into the Caste of a Thief ||

If the "Banner" <note>Dhvaja</note> income-measure is present, the thief is a Priest. If "Smoke" <note>Dhumra</note>, the thief is a Warrior. If "Lion" <note>Simha</note>, the thief is a Merchant. If "Donkey" <note>Khara</note>, the thief is a servant. || 11 ||

If "Elephant" <note>Gaja</note>, the thief is a maidservant. If "Crow" <note>Dhvanksha</note>, the thief is a leader or manager. If "Bull" <note>Vrisha</note> or "Dog" <note>Shvana</note>, the thief is from the <term>Antyaja-sambhava</term> <note>literally "born of the last"; historically referring to those outside the four-tier caste system</note>. || 12 ||

**Prose Construction:** If at the time of inquiry the Banner income-measure is found, the thief is of the Priest caste. In Smoke, the thief is of the Warrior caste. In Lion, the thief is of the Merchant caste. In Donkey, the thief is a servant of the house or office. In Elephant, the thief is a maidservant or domestic worker. In Crow, the thief is a manager, accountant, agent, or representative. In Bull or Dog, the thief is from the lowest social classes. || 11-12 ||

---

## || Knowledge of the Direction of Lost Objects ||

In the Banner, the object has gone East; in Smoke, it has gone Southeast. In the Lion, the object is in the South; in the Dog, it is in the Southwest. || 13 ||

In the Bull, it is known to be in the West; in the Donkey, in the Northwest. In the Elephant, the property is in the North; in the Crow, it is in the Northeast. || 14 ||

**Prose Construction:** In the Banner income-measure, the lost object is missing in the East. In Smoke, the lost object goes to the Southeast. In Lion, it is in the South; in Dog, in the Southwest; in Bull, in the West; in Donkey, in the Northwest; in Elephant, in the North; and in Crow, the lost object is in the Northeast. || 13-14 ||

## || Knowledge of the Location of Lost Objects ||

In the Banner, the object is lost in an upper story or high place. In Smoke, it is in the kitchen or fire-room. In the Lion, it has gone to the forest or wilderness. In the Dog, it is in another location entirely. || 15 ||

If the *Vrusha* <note>Bull</note> Aya is present, the lost item is in a grassy meadow; if *Khara* <note>Donkey</note>, it is in any of the four directions around the village. If *Gaja* <note>Elephant</note>, it should be known to be on a riverbank; if *Dhvanksha* <note>Crow</note>, one should say it is atop a tree. || 16 ||

**Prose Order:** — If *Dhvaja* <note>Flag</note>, the item is in barren land; if *Dhuma* <note>Smoke</note>, it is lost in a house of fire. If *Simha* <note>Lion</note>, it is in the forest; likewise, if *Shvana* <note>Dog</note>, it has gone to another location. || 15 ||
If *Vrusha*, in a meadow; if *Khara*, in the four directions of the village; if *Gaja*, it is known to be on a riverbank; if *Dhvanksha*, one should say it is atop a tree. || 16 ||

**Commentary:** — If at the time of the query the *Dhvaja* Aya appears, the lost (missing) object is on high ground. In the *Dhuma* Aya, the lost object is in or near a fire-house, kitchen, brick kiln, etc. In the *Simha* Aya, the object is in a forest, and in the *Shvana* Aya, the object is in another house belonging to the homeowner or in a neighbor's house.

In the *Vrusha* Aya, the lost object is in a pasture; in the *Khara* Aya, it is in the lands surrounding the village; in the *Gaja* Aya, it is on a riverbank or in a carriage house (garage), etc.; and in the *Dhvanksha* Aya, one should search for the lost item in a high place such as a tree. || 15-16 ||

### || Inquiry into the Welfare of a Traveler ||

In *Dhvaja*, *Simha*, *Vrusha*, and *Kunjara* <note>Elephant</note>, there will be well-being. In *Dhvanksha*, *Shvana*, *Khara*, and *Dhuma*, one should say there is no well-being. || 17 ||

**Prose Order:** — In *Dhvaja*, *Simha*, *Vrusha*, and *Kunjara*, there will be well-being. In *Dhvanksha*, *Shvana*, *Khara*, and *Dhuma*, one should say there is no well-being. || 17 ||

**Commentary:** — At the time of the query, based on the first letter of the name of a fruit or flower mentioned by the seeker, or the first letter of the query itself: if the *Dhvaja*, *Simha*, *Vrusha*, or *Gaja* Ayas are present, then in a query regarding a traveler's welfare, the traveler is safe. However, in the *Dhvanksha*, *Shvana*, *Khara*, or *Dhuma* Ayas, there is no well-being. || 17 ||

### || Inquiry into the Traveler's Stability or Movement ||

In *Dhvaja* and *Gaja*, he is stationary; in *Shvana* and *Simha*, he is restless. In *Vrusha* and *Dhuma*, he is prepared for travel; in *Khara* and *Dhanksha*, he is in distress. || 18 ||

**Prose Order:** — In *Dhvaja* and *Gaja*, he is indeed stationary; in *Shvana* and *Simha*, he is restless; in *Vrusha* and *Dhuma*, he is prepared for travel; in *Khara* and *Dhvanksha*, he is in distress <note>original: "kantakah", literally 'a thorn', implying obstacles or pain</note>. || 18 ||

**Commentary:** — If the query asks whether a traveler has left their location or not: if the *Dhvaja* or *Gaja* Ayas appear, one should say the traveler is stationary. In the *Shvana* and *Simha* Ayas, he is wandering through many places. In the *Vrusha* and *Dhuma* Ayas, he is preparing to depart, and if the *Khara* or *Dhvanksha* Ayas appear, the traveler is trapped in a crisis such as illness or enemies. || 18 ||

### || Knowledge of the Distance of a Traveler or Enemy Army ||

In *Dhvaja* and *Dhuma*, they are nearby; in *Gaja* and *Simha*, they are far away. In *Vrusha* and *Khara*, they are on the path; in *Dhvanksha* and *Shvana*, they have departed again. || 19 ||

<note>Technical vocabulary used: Dhvaja (Flag), Dhuma (Smoke), Simha (Lion), Shvana (Dog), Vrusha (Bull), Khara (Donkey), Gaja (Elephant), Dhvanksha (Crow). These represent the eight 'Ayas' or mathematical remainders used to determine auspiciousness.</note>

## Compendium of Kerala Horary Astrology

**Connection:** If the *Dhvaja* (Flag) or *Dhumra* (Smoke) categories appear, the subject is nearby; in *Gaja* (Elephant) and *Simha* (Lion), they are far away; in *Vrisha* (Bull) and *Khara* (Donkey), they are on the path; in *Dhvanksha* (Crow) and *Shvana* (Dog), they have turned back. || 19 ||

**Richa Commentary:** When the question is asked regarding how far a traveler, a guest, or an enemy army is, and if the *Dhvaja* or *Dhumra* categories are active, one should say they are very close to home. In the *Gaja* and *Simha* categories, say they are far away. In *Vrisha* and *Khara*, they are midway on the journey, and in *Dhvanksha* and *Shvana*, it should be said that they have turned back from the path to their original location. || 19 ||

Nowadays, with telecommunication tools like mobile phones, one can instantly find out where a guest or a wedding procession is. However, in ancient times, astrology was the only support, and such questions were asked frequently.

### || Questions Regarding the Duration of Work ||

In *Dhvaja*, a fortnight is declared; in *Dhumra*, seven days; twenty-one days in *Simha*, and likewise a month in *Shvana*. || 20 ||
In *Vrisha*, a month and a half; in *Khara*, two months; in *Gaja*, three months are declared; and in *Dhvanksha*, the duration of an *Ayana* <note>An Ayana is a period of six months, representing the sun's half-yearly course.</note>. || 21 ||

**Connection:** In *Dhvaja*, a fortnight; in *Dhumra*, seven days; in *Simha*, twenty-one; in *Shvana*, a month; in *Vrisha*, a month and a half; in *Khara*, two months; in *Gaja*, three months; and in *Dhvanksha*, an *Ayana* is declared. || 20-21 ||

**Richa Commentary:** When the question is: "In how many days will this specific task be completed?" or "In how much time will the visitor arrive?", the answer is as follows:

If the *Dhvaja* category is present at the time of the query, it takes one fortnight (15 days); in *Dhumra*, a week; in *Simha*, three weeks; in *Shvana*, one month; in *Vrisha*, one and a half months; in *Khara*, two months; in *Gaja*, three months; and in *Dhvanksha*, an entire *Ayana* (six months) of time is required. || 20-21 ||

### || Questions Regarding Mineral, Vegetable, or Animal Matters ||

In *Dhvaja* and *Dhumra*, there is concern for <term>Dhatu</term> <note>Inorganic matter/minerals</note>; in *Gaja* and *Simha*, <term>Mula</term> <note>Vegetable matter/roots</note>; in *Shvana*, *Khara*, *Vrisha*, and *Dhvanksha*, the wise one should speak of <term>Jiva</term> <note>Living beings/animals/humans</note>. || 22 ||

**Connection:** In *Dhvaja* and *Dhumra*, concern for minerals; in *Gaja* and *Simha*, roots; in *Shvana*, *Khara*, *Vrisha*, and *Dhvanksha*, the wise one should state concern for living beings. || 22 ||

**Richa Commentary:** In the case of a "silent question" <note>Muka Prashna: where the querent does not state their worry aloud</note>, if the query results in *Dhvaja* or *Dhumra*, the astrologer should tell the querent their worry relates to minerals or metals. In *Gaja* and *Simha*, the worry relates to plants or roots. If any of the remaining—*Shvana*, *Khara*, *Vrisha*, or *Dhvanksha*—appear, the astrologer should state that the concern is regarding a living being. || 22 ||

### || Knowledge of Metals ||

In *Dhvaja*, gold is to be known; in *Dhumra*, likewise silver; in *Simha*, copper is to be known; and in *Shvana*, likewise iron. || 23 ||

**Connection of Words—** In the Banner, gold; likewise in the Smoke, silver is to be known. In the Lion, copper; likewise in the Dog, iron is to be understood. In the Bull, bronze; in the Donkey, tin <note>original: "naga", here referring to tin</note>; in the Elephant, lead is declared. In the Crow, brass is stated by the best of calculators. || 23-24 ||

### || Knowledge of the Color of Objects in Fist-Divination ||

->In the Banner, it is safflower-colored; in the Smoke, it is known as white.<-
->In the Lion, it is reddish; in the Dog, it is pale-blue. || 27 ||<-
->In the Bull, it is known as yellow; in the Donkey, it is of mixed colors.<-
->In the Elephant, it is dark-hued; in the Crow, it is again mixed. || 28 ||<-

**Prose Order:** In the Banner *Aya*, the color is safflower; likewise in the Smoke, it is known as white. In the Lion, it is reddish; in the Dog, it is pale-blue. In the Bull, it is yellow; in the Donkey, it is known as mixed-colored. In the Elephant, it is dark, and in the Crow, it is again mixed. || 27-28 ||

**Commentary:** When someone holds an object in their fist and asks, "What object is in my hand?" <note>This refers to *Mushti-Prashna*, or "fist-inquiry," a common test of a diviner's skill.</note> then the color of the object according to the *Aya* <note>Aya refers to eight mathematical residues used to determine directions and characteristics.</note> is as follows: In the Banner *Aya*, the object in the fist is the color of a safflower <note>original: "kusuma," here referring to the orange-red of the safflower plant</note>. In the Smoke *Aya*, the object is white. In the Lion *Aya*, it is red. In the Dog *Aya*, it is a mixed color of pale-white and blue. In the Bull, it is yellow, and in the Donkey *Aya*, it is a mixed (variegated) color. In the Elephant *Aya*, it is a dark or blackish hue, and in the Crow, it is mixed like the donkey. || 27-28 ||

### || Knowledge of the Category of the Object in the Fist ||

->In the Banner, it is a leaf; in the Smoke, it is declared a flower.<-
->In the Lion, it is a fruit; in the Dog, it is wood and such. || 29 ||<-
->In the Bull, it is grain; in the Donkey, it is called grass.<-
->In the Elephant, it is a seed; in the Crow, it is remembered as chaff. || 30 ||<-

**Prose Order:** In the Banner, a leaf is to be known; in the Smoke, a flower is proclaimed. In the Lion, a fruit; and in the Dog, wood etc. is to be known. In the Bull, grain is spoken of; likewise in the Donkey, grass is mentioned. In the Elephant, a seed is to be known; and in the Crow, chaff is remembered. || 29-30 ||

**Commentary:** Now the category of the object is described. If it is the Banner *Aya*, the object is the leaf of a plant. In Smoke, it is a flower. In the Lion *Aya*, it is a fruit. In the Dog, it is wood, and in the Donkey *Aya*, the object in the fist is a blade of grass or similar. In the Elephant *Aya*, the object should be known as the seed of some plant, and in the *Aya* named Crow, it is chaff. Chaff is defined as the husk of grain. || 29-30 ||

## ## Inquiry Regarding Son or Daughter ##

> If the Flag, Bull, Elephant, or Lion appears, one should predict a son for the pregnant woman.
> If the Smoke, Dog, Donkey, or Crow appears, one should specify the birth of a daughter. || 31 ||

**Prose Order:** If the Flag, Bull, Elephant, or Lion appears, one should predict a son for the pregnant woman. If the Smoke, Dog, Donkey, or Crow appears, one should specify the birth of a daughter. || 31 ||

**Commentary:** In a divination inquiry, if any of these four <term>Ayas</term> <note>Aya refers to a specific mathematical remainder used in Vedic astrology to determine the character of a space or person</note>—<term>Flag</term>, <term>Bull</term>, <term>Elephant</term>, or <term>Lion</term>—are present, it should be said that the pregnant woman will give birth to a son. If the <term>Smoke</term>, <term>Dog</term>, <term>Donkey</term>, or <term>Crow</term> Ayas occur, the birth of a daughter should be indicated. || 31 ||

---

## ## Knowledge of Life Expectancy (Longevity) ##

> For the Flag and Lion, one hundred years are declared; for the Elephant, it is "Sky-Elephant" <note>original: "Vyomagaja," a numerical code where Sky=0 and Elephant=8, meaning 80</note>.
> For the Bull, sixty years; for the Donkey, it is known as "Sky-Ocean" <note>original: "Vyomabdhi," where Sky=0 and Ocean=4, meaning 40</note>. || 32 ||
> For the Dog, twenty is declared; for the Crow, sixteen.
> For the Smoke, it is known as one year; thus should one contemplate longevity. || 33 ||

**Prose Order:** For the Flag and Lion, one hundred (years); likewise for the Elephant, <term>Sky-Elephant</term> is declared. For the Bull, sixty; for the Donkey, <term>Sky-Ocean</term> years. For the Dog, twenty; likewise for the Crow, sixteen is declared. For the Smoke, it should be known as one year; thus should one contemplate longevity. || 32-33 ||

**Commentary:** When the <term>Flag Aya</term> appears in an inquiry, the lifespan is one hundred years. When the <term>Elephant Aya</term> appears, the lifespan is eighty years. In the <term>Bull Aya</term>, the lifespan is sixty years. In the <term>Donkey Aya</term>, the lifespan is forty years. In the <term>Dog Aya</term>, the lifespan is twenty years. In the <term>Crow Aya</term>, the lifespan is sixteen years. In the <term>Smoke Aya</term>, the lifespan is the shortest, being only one year. || 32-33 ||

When a petitioner asks a question regarding longevity, the answer should be given based on these verses. However, while answering, one should state that the lifespan of the petitioner (or the subject of the inquiry) will fall between such-and-such years. For example, in the Smoke Aya, between 1 and 16 years; in the Crow Aya, between sixteen and twenty.

---

## ## Inquiry Regarding Victory and Defeat ##

> In the Flag, Elephant, Bull, and Lion, the "Stayers" achieve victory.
> In the Smoke, Dog, Donkey, and Crow, one should predict victory for the "Movers." || 34 ||

**Prose Order:** In the Flag, Elephant, Bull, and Lion, the <term>Stayers</term> achieve victory. In the Smoke, Dog, Donkey, and Crow, one should predict victory for the <term>Movers</term>. || 34 ||

**Commentary:** When there is a question of victory or defeat, if the <term>Flag</term>, <term>Elephant</term>, <term>Bull</term>, or <term>Lion</term> Ayas are present, the <term>Stayer</term> <note>original: "Sthayi," referring to the defender or the resident party</note> wins. If the <term>Smoke</term>, <term>Dog</term>, <term>Donkey</term>, or <term>Crow</term> Ayas are present, the <term>Mover</term> <note>original: "Yaayi," referring to the attacker or the traveling party</note> wins. || 34 ||

<summary>This page details how to use the eight Ayas to predict the gender of an unborn child, the life expectancy of an individual, and the outcome of a conflict between a defender and an attacker.</summary>
<keywords>Aya, divination, longevity, gender prediction, victory and defeat, Vedic astrology, Sthayi, Yaayi</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the technical instructions for Prashna (horary astrology) from the Kerala tradition, specifically focusing on the classification of opposing parties in conflicts and the verification of rumors.</meta>

<page-num>56</page-num>
<header>Compendium of Kerala Horary Astrology</header>

In lawsuits, wars, disputes, and sports matches, the decision of victory or defeat should be made using this verse. The explanation of the *Yayi* <note>The Attacker/Mover</note> and the *Sthayi* <note>The Defender/Stationary party</note> is given below—

**Yayi—** (1) In war, the king, commander, or army that initiates the attack or invasion is called the *Yayi*. This is also known as the "Advancer." In English, this is called the **Attacker**.

(2) In any civil or property lawsuit, the petitioner or applicant—known in Arabic as the *Mudda'i* <note>original: "मुद्दई"</note> and in English as the **Applicant**—is also called the *Yayi*. This party is also known as the Plaintiff.

(3) In criminal cases, the petitioner or complainant, known in English as the **Plaintiff**, is also the *Yayi*.

(4) In sports, the team that throws the ball first is the *Yayi*.

**Sthayi—** (1) This is also called the "Lord of the City" or "Lord of the Town." One who remains in their own place is called the *Sthayi*. In war, the king, head of state, nation, or military side that is attacked is called the *Sthayi*. This is called *Mudda-alayhi* <note>original: "मुद्गॅ" - likely a corruption of the Arabic 'Mudda-alayhi' meaning Defendant</note> in Arabic and **Defender** in English. This is the party under attack. This is the *Sthayi*.

(2) In criminal cases, the counter-petitioner or respondent, known in English as the **Defendant**, is also called the *Sthayi*. This is the defending side.

(3) In civil and property lawsuits, the respondent is called the **Non-Applicant** in English; they are also the *Sthayi*.

(4) In sports, the side (team) that catches or returns the ball is the *Sthayi*.

(5) Similarly, in a debate, the party answering the questions is the *Sthayi*, and the one who initiates the questioning is the *Yayi*.

->|| Inquiry into the Truth or Falsehood of Rumors ||<-

> If the omen falls under the Banner, Elephant, Lion, or Bull, the rumor is true.
> If it falls under the Dog, Donkey, Crow, or Smoke, the rumor is false. || 35 ||
<note>This verse uses the 'Ashta-Aya' or eight-fold income/orientation system to determine the validity of news.</note>

**Prose Order:** In a query, if the *Aya* <note>astrological orientation</note> is the Banner, Elephant, Lion, or Bull, the rumor is true. If the *Aya* is the Dog, Donkey, Crow, or Smoke, the rumor is false. || 35 ||

**Commentary:** If someone asks whether a particular rumor or hearsay is true or false, and at the time of the query, one of the *Ayas*—Banner, Elephant, Lion, or Bull—is present, then the rumor (*Upashruti*) is true. However, if the *Aya* is the Dog, Donkey, Smoke, or Crow, then that rumor (public report or hearsay) is false. || 35 ||

<summary>This page defines the astrological roles of 'Attacker' (Yayi) and 'Defender' (Sthayi) across legal, military, and sporting contexts, and provides a formula for determining if a rumor is true based on eight symbolic categories.</summary>
<keywords>Yayi, Sthayi, Attacker, Defender, Lawsuit, War, Rumors, Kerala Prashna Shastra, Ashta-Aya, Omen verification</keywords>
