### || Inquiry Regarding Rain ||

In the signs of the Smoke, the Bull, the Elephant, and the Dog, there is excellent rain.
In the Banner and the Lion, there is a delay; in the Donkey and the Crow, there is no rain. || 36 ||

**Prose Construction:** In the Smoke, Elephant, Bull, and Dog, the rain is excellent. In the Banner and the Lion, there is a delay. In the Donkey and the Crow, there is no rain. || 36 ||

**Commentary:** When a question is asked regarding rainfall, if the *Aya* <note>The "Aya" refers to one of the eight mathematical residues used in Indian architecture and divination to determine the character of a moment or structure.</note> results in Smoke, Elephant, Bull, or Dog, the rainfall will be excellent. If the *Aya* is the Banner or the Lion, the rain will be delayed. In the Donkey and Crow *Ayas*, there is no rainfall at all. || 36 ||

---

### || Duration of Rainy Days ||

For Smoke, seven days are declared; for the Bull, it is ten.
For the Dog, twenty should be known; for the Elephant, twenty-seven. || 37 ||
For the Lion and the Banner, it is forty; for the Donkey and the Crow, a season.
This is the sequence declared by the best of astrologers for the rainy season. || 38 ||

**Prose Construction:** In Smoke, seven days; likewise in the Bull, ten <note>original: "digbhih" (literally 'the directions'), a numerical code for 10</note>. In the Dog, twenty; in the Elephant, twenty-seven should be known. In the Lion and the Banner, forty <note>original: "vyomabdhi" (literally 'sky-ocean'), a numerical code for 40</note>; likewise in the Donkey and the Crow, a season <note>original: "rtu", representing 2 months</note>. This sequence has been stated by the best of astrologers for the rainy season. || 37-38 ||

**Commentary:** How many days will it rain? For this, the great astrologers have explained this sequence: If the question is asked during a Smoke *Aya*, it will rain for seven days. In a Bull *Aya*, the rainy days are ten. In a Dog *Aya*, it rains for twenty days. In an Elephant *Aya*, the measure of rain is twenty-seven days. If it is a Lion or Banner *Aya*, the measure is 40 (forty) days. If the rain inquiry occurs during the Donkey or Crow *Ayas*, it rains for a full season (2 months). || 37-38 ||

---

### || Inquiry Regarding Gain or Loss of a Wife ||

In the Banner, the Lion, and the Bull, one quickly
Obtains a beautiful and well-behaved wife.
In the Dog, Elephant, Crow, Donkey, and Smoke,
There is loss of the objective and likewise strife. || 39 ||

**Prose Construction:** In the Banner, Lion, and Bull, one quickly obtains a beautiful, well-behaved wife. In the Dog, Elephant, Crow, Donkey, and Smoke, there is loss of the work/objective and likewise strife. || 39 ||

**Commentary:** If a question is asked regarding the gain or acquisition of a wife, and at that time the *Aya* is the Banner, Lion, or Bull, then one will quickly obtain a beautiful and virtuous wife. If the *Aya* is the Dog, Elephant, Crow, Donkey, or Smoke, there is a loss of the objective—meaning a wife is not obtained—and there will be quarreling. || 39 ||

### ### The Inquiry Regarding Business and Relationships

> ->In the signs of the Banner, Elephant, Bull, and Lion, dealings are auspicious.<-
> ->In the Crow, Dog, Donkey, and Smoke, they bring strife and inauspiciousness. (40)<-

**Connection:** In the Banner, Elephant, Bull, and Lion, dealings are auspicious. In the Crow, Dog, Donkey, and Smoke, they provide inauspicious results such as quarrels. (40)

**Commentary:** Transactions, contacts, relationships, and friendships constitute "dealings" <note>Sanskrit: Vyavahara</note>. When an inquiry is made and the calculated <term>Aya</term> <note>A mathematical remainder used in Indian architecture and astrology to determine the character of a person or building</note> results in the Banner, Elephant, Bull, or Lion, one should predict that dealings with the person in question will be beneficial, as these results grant auspicious fruits. However, if the Crow, Dog, Donkey, or Smoke results appear, the outcome of the inquiry regarding dealings will be discord, fighting, and disputes. (40)

---

### ### The Inquiry Regarding Attaining Power or Kingdom

> ->With the Elephant and Banner, attainment comes after a long time; with the Bull and Lion, there is speed.<-
> ->With the Dog and Donkey, there is no attainment; the enemy surely seizes it. (41)<-
> ->With the Crow and Smoke, there is no position, and there is strife with brothers.<-
> ->This has been stated by the best of astrologers in the considerations of Royal Yoga. (42)<-

**Connection:** In the considerations of <term>Rajayoga</term> <note>Combinations for power, success, or royal status</note>, it is said by the best of astrologers that with the Elephant and Banner, the attainment of the kingdom occurs after a long time; with the Bull and Lion, there is speed; with the Dog and Donkey, there is certainly no attainment and the enemy seizes it. With the Crow and Smoke, there is no royal position and there is strife with brothers. (41-42)

**Commentary:** When a question is asked regarding obtaining a kingdom, authority, or a specific post—or regarding success in an election—if the result is the Elephant or Banner, the position is attained after a long delay. If the result is the Bull or Lion, the position is attained quickly, or success is achieved in that very election. In the Dog or Donkey results, the position is not attained; rather, the seeker or their party suffers defeat at the hands of the enemy or remains unsuccessful. If the Crow or Smoke results appear, the position will not be attained due to infighting with "brothers" <note>metaphorically: colleagues or party members</note>, or failure occurs due to internal betrayal during the election. Great astrologers have spoken thus regarding the consideration of power. (41-42)

---

### ### The Inquiry Regarding the Safety of a Ship

> ->In the Banner, Elephant, Lion, and Bull, the ship is safe.<-
> ->In the Crow, Smoke, Donkey, and Dog, the ship surely sinks. (43)<-

**Connection:** In the Banner, Elephant, Lion, and Bull, the ship is endowed with safety. In the Crow, Smoke, Donkey, and Dog, the ship surely sinks. (43)

**Commentary:** When a ship has been sent for trade via sea routes, or <insert>in a modern context</insert> a truck has gone to deliver or collect goods, the answer to an inquiry regarding its safety and well-being should be given as follows:

If the letters of the inquiry result in the Banner, Elephant, Lion, or Bull, then the ship or truck etc. [is safe]...

### || Inquiry Regarding Obtaining Authority ||

**If the result is Flag or Elephant, authority is gained after a long time; if Bull or Lion, it is gained quickly.**
**There is conflict if it is Donkey or Dog, and no gain if it is Crow or Smoke. || 44 ||**

**Connection of words:** If *Dhwaja* <note>Flag</note> or *Gaja* <note>Elephant</note>, gain occurs after a long time. If *Vrisha* <note>Bull</note> or *Simha* <note>Lion</note>, gain is quick. If *Khara* <note>Donkey</note> or *Shwana* <note>Dog</note>, there is conflict. In *Dhwanksha* <note>Crow</note> and *Dhumra* <note>Smoke</note>, there is no gain. || 44 ||

**Commentary:** When a question is asked regarding taking possession of a specific post, land, or property: if the *Aaya* is Flag or Elephant, authority will be obtained late. Bull or Lion *Aayas* grant authority quickly. If the *Aaya* is Donkey or Dog, there will be a dispute or quarrel. If the *Aaya* is Crow or Smoke, authority is not obtained at all. || 44 ||

### || Inquiry Regarding Obtaining a Village or Land ||

**In Flag, Elephant, Bull, and Lion, the acquisition of a village certainly occurs.**
**In Dog, Donkey, Crow, and Smoke, it is certain that there is no acquisition. || 45 ||**

**Connection of words:** In *Dhwaja*, *Gaja*, *Vrisha*, or *Simha*, the acquisition of a village will certainly happen. In *Shwana*, *Khara*, *Dhwanksha*, and *Dhumra*, it is certain that it will not happen. || 45 ||

**Commentary:** If, according to the previously mentioned method, the *Aaya* is Flag, Elephant, Bull, or Lion, then the acquisition of a village etc. will definitely occur. However, if the *Aaya* is Dog, Donkey, Crow, or Smoke, the village etc. is not obtained. || 45 ||

### || Inquiry Regarding Success in Work ||

**In Flag and Elephant, work is done after a long time; in Bull and Lion, it is quick.**
**In Donkey and Dog, it takes a very long time; in Crow and Smoke, it is not accomplished. || 46 ||**

**Connection of words:** In *Dhwaja* or *Gaja*, work is done after a long time; in *Vrisha* and *Simha*, it is quick; in *Khara* and *Shwana*, it happens over a long period. In *Dhwanksha* and *Dhumra*, it is not accomplished. || 46 ||

**Commentary:** If the question concerns the success of a task: Flag and Elephant *Aayas* indicate success after a long duration. In Bull and Lion *Aayas*, the work is completed quickly. If the *Aaya* is Donkey or Dog, the work is successful only after a very long time. However, if the *Aaya* is Crow or Smoke, the work is never accomplished. || 46 ||

### || Inquiry Regarding the Release of a Prisoner ||

->In the signs of Smoke, Dog, Donkey, or Crow, the prisoner is quickly released.<-
->In the signs of Banner, Elephant, Bull, or Lion, one should predict the hardship of the prisoner. || 47 ||<-

**Connection—** In Smoke, Dog, Donkey, or (alternatively) Crow, the prisoner is quickly released. In Banner, Elephant, Bull, or (alternatively) Lion, one should predict the hardship of the prisoner. || 47 ||

**Commentary—** When an inquiry is made regarding a person thrown into prison, or a person detained in police custody, or a person fallen into the clutches of kidnappers: if the <term>Aya</term><note>Aya refers to a specific mathematical remainder used in Indian architecture and astrology to determine the character of a point in space or time.</note> results in Smoke, Dog, Donkey, or Crow, then that person will be quickly freed from the clutches of the prison, police, or dacoits. Conversely, if the result is Banner, Elephant, Bull, or Lion, the prisoner will remain in distress. It will be possible for them to be released only with great difficulty. || 47 ||

In this specific context, the "auspicious" results are painful for the prisoner, while the "inauspicious" results grant the prisoner's release. This confirms the principle of the Great Sage Parashara:

> "Benefic planets do not grant auspicious results to men if they are lords of the angles; likewise, malefic planets do not grant inauspicious results if they are lords of the angles." <note>original: "न दिशन्ति शुभं नृणां सौम्या: केन्द्राधिपा यदि । क्रूराश्चेदशुभम् ।"</note>

This means that naturally gentle planets do not give good results when they own the central houses (1st, 4th, 7th, 10th), and naturally harsh planets do not give bad results when they own those same houses.

---

### || Worship of Deities for the Accomplishment of Tasks ||

->In the Banner, there should be worship of Bhairava; in Smoke, the Mother of the World.<-
->In the Lion, one should worship the Sun; and in the Dog, the Son of the Wind. || 48 ||<-
->In the Bull, the worship of Shiva; and in the Donkey, the Goddess of Speech.<-
->In the one called the Great Elephant, Ganesha; and in the Crow, the worship of the Ancestors. || 49 ||<-

**Connection—** In the Banner, there should be worship of Bhairava, and in Smoke, one should worship the Mother of the World. In the Lion, the Sun, and in the Dog, one should worship the Son of the Wind. In the Bull, the worship of Shiva; likewise in the Donkey, the Goddess of Speech; in the one called the Great Elephant, Ganesha; and in the Crow, the worship of the Ancestors. || 48-49 ||

**Commentary—** When someone asks, "Which deity should I worship for the success of my work?", the answer should be given according to the <term>Aya</term> obtained from the first letter of the seeker's name, or the first letter of the name of a fruit or flower they have presented.

If the result is the **Banner**, success is achieved by worshiping Bhairava <note>A fierce manifestation of Shiva</note>. If it is **Smoke**, worshiping the Mother of the World (Jagadamba) is desired. In the **Lion**, worship the Sun. In the **Dog**, worship Hanuman, the Son of the Wind. In the **Bull**, perform the worship of Shiva. In the **Donkey**, worship Saraswati, the Goddess of Speech. In the **Elephant**, success is achieved by worshiping Lord Ganesha. In the result known as the **Crow**, success is found by worshiping the Ancestors (Pitrus). || 48-49 ||

# Now Begins the Science of Body Movements Spoken by Narada

I shall now proclaim the Science of Body Movements <note>original: "Angavidya"</note> composed by Narada himself. By the mere touch of a limb, one can indeed know what is auspicious or inauspicious. || 1 ||
**Prose Order:**—I shall proclaim the Science of Body Movements composed by Narada himself. By which, through the mere touch of a limb, the auspicious and inauspicious is to be known. || 1 ||
**Commentary:**—According to the compiler, this science of body movements was composed by the sage Narada himself. Through this knowledge, the knowledge of auspicious or inauspicious results is gained simply by the act of touching a limb. || 1 ||
**Special Note:**—This science, related to a person's physical gestures, clearly reveals their inner emotions. Results manifest in the external world according to the fruits of these inner feelings. Modern palmists <note>original: "Samudrika Shastris"</note> and psychologists have shed much light on this subject and used this science in "personality" analysis. || 1 ||

->|| The Results of Touching the Upper Limbs ||<-

If the head is touched when one asks a question, there will be great gain. The gain of gold, wealth, and grain will surely be his. || 2 ||
**Prose Order:**—If the head is touched while asking, there will be great gain. Then, the gain of gold, wealth, and grain will certainly be his. || 2 ||
**Commentary:**—When an inquirer touches their own head while questioning an astrologer, they will certainly gain gold, wealth, grain, and so on. || 2 ||

When a person asks while touching the mouth, the nose, the eyes, or the ears, then one should predict a gain. || 3 ||
**Prose Order:**—When a person asks while touching the mouth, the nose, the eyes, and the ears, then one should point out a gain. || 3 ||
**Commentary:**—When the inquiring person asks a question while touching their mouth, nose, eyes, ears, etc., then it should be said that the person will experience a gain. || 3 ||

**Statement:**—In verses 2-3, the result of touching the organs located in the head is said to be extremely auspicious and a bringer of prosperity. The reason for this is that the head is the "supreme limb" <note>original: "Uttamanga"</note> of the body. A person's identity is also established by it. The life-force of living beings and all the senses remain dependent and protected within it; therefore, among all the limbs of the body, it is the superior limb—
> "That in which the life-breaths of the living and all the senses are situated, and which is the best of all limbs, is called the Head." <note>original: "Pranah pranabhritam yatrashritah sarvendriyani cha..."</note>
—Charaka Samhita, Sutra Sthana, 17/10 <note>A foundational text of Ayurveda, the ancient Indian system of medicine.</note>

<summary>This page introduces Angavidya, the divinatory science of interpreting body gestures, specifically noting that touching the head or facial features during an inquiry signals impending wealth and success.</summary>
<keywords>Angavidya, Narada, divination, body language, Charaka Samhita, astrology, auspicious signs, head gestures</keywords>

<page-num>64</page-num>
<header>Compendium of Kerala Prashna Shastra</header>

For this reason, touching the head or its appendages—the nose, ears, eyes, mouth, etc.—is an indicator of auspiciousness.

### || Results of Touching the Middle Limbs ||

->If the neck, the shoulder, the throat, or the arms are touched,<-
->And the querent asks a question, their gain will be only slight. || 4 ||<-

**Word-for-word connection:** If the querent <term>Prichhaka</term> <note>the person asking the astrological question</note> touches the neck, shoulder, throat, and arms while asking a question, there will be only a small amount of gain for them. || 4 ||

**Richa Commentary:** When the querent asks a question while touching the neck, shoulders, throat, or arms, then whatever object or matter they are inquiring about will result in only a small degree of profit or success. || 4 ||

->Touching the stomach or the base of the navel, he who asks for himself,<-
->Food and drink shall be his, and his agricultural work will succeed. || 5 ||<-

**Word-for-word connection:** He who asks while touching his own stomach or the base of the navel, it is established that he will obtain food and drink through agricultural work. || 5 ||

**Richa Commentary:** If a querent asks a question while touching their own stomach or the area below the navel, then food and water will be produced at their home, and their livelihood will be sustained specifically through farming. || 5 ||

In the body of the <term>Virat Purusha</term> <note>the "Universal Man" or cosmic giant whose body represents the universe and social order</note>, the stomach and related areas are the place of the <term>Vaishyas</term> <note>the merchant and agricultural class</note>, and for them, agricultural work is a natural certainty.

->If the querent should touch the waist, the penis, or the thighs,<-
->The gain of a daughter and the wealth of a son shall be theirs. || 6 ||<-

**Word-for-word connection:** If the querent touches the waist, the penis, and the thighs, then they shall have the gain of a daughter and the wealth of a son. || 6 ||

**Richa Commentary:** If the questioner asks a question while touching their waist, penis <note>original: "shishna"</note>, or thighs, they will benefit from the birth of both daughters and sons. || 6 ||

Touching parts related to the reproductive organs is an indicator of obtaining progeny. In verses 4, 5, and 6, the result of touching the middle parts of the body is of a "medium" quality, yet success is definitely achieved in these matters. After the head, the importance of these locations is worth keeping in mind from the perspective of health <term>Arogya</term>; because besides the head, the <term>Pranas</term> <note>vital life forces</note> also reside in the heart and the bladder (urinary bladder + kidneys)—

> "The vital breaths of men are established in the heart, the head, and the bladder.
> Therefore, one should always make an effort to protect them."
>
> — Charaka Samhita, Siddhi Sthana, Chapter 8, Verse 9

## || Results of Touching the Lower Limbs ||

> If one should touch the knees, the shanks, the ankles, or the feet,
> The querent shall face death or great distress; of this, there is no doubt. || 7 ||

**Prose Order:** If [the querent] touches the knees, shanks, ankles, or feet, then for that querent, death or distress shall surely occur; there is no doubt. || 7 ||

**Commentary:** If the person asking the question touches their knees, calves, ankles, or feet while posing the query, they will undoubtedly face suffering, death, or pain equivalent to death. || 7 ||

<note>The text refers to the lower body as "Adhamanga," literally meaning the "inferior or base limbs." In traditional Indian physiognomy and divination, touching these parts during a consultation is considered an inauspicious omen.</note>

## || Results of a Query Accompanied by Offerings ||

> Taking fruit, flowers, or new cloth, if one asks,
> To that all-knowing one, the querent’s success arises. || 8 ||

**Prose Order:** If one asks while holding fruit, flowers, or new cloth, then for that querent, everything results in successful outcomes. || 8 ||

**Commentary:** When a querent asks a question while bringing offerings such as fruit, flowers, or new clothing <term>Dakshina: a ritual gift or fee given to a teacher or priest</term>, then as soon as they ask, their desires are fulfilled and their work succeeds due to the rise of their past merits. The words of the astrologer prove true. || 8 ||

## || Results of a Query with Forbidden Objects ||

> Taking charcoal, grass, and such, if one asks,
> No success in that work arises, even with great effort. || 9 ||

**Prose Order:** If one asks while holding charcoal, grass, etc., then despite efforts in the work, success will not be achieved for them. || 9 ||

**Commentary:** One who asks a question while holding coal, shards, or straw in their hand will find that their work does not succeed, even after a hundred thousand efforts. || 9 ||

<note>The text emphasizes that a question asked without a proper offering, or with a "forbidden" offering, is always unsuccessful. It notes metaphorically that "Dakshina" (the offering) is the wife of "Yajna" (sacrifice/ritual), implying that the ritual is incomplete without it.</note>

->Likewise<-

> Taking a weapon, wood, or a [foul] scent, if one asks,
> Agitation shall always be theirs, and planetary affliction arises. || 10 ||

**Prose Order:** If one asks while holding a weapon, wood, and a scent, they will face constant agitation and planetary defects will arise. || 10 ||

**Commentary:** If the querent asks a question while holding a weapon, wood (such as a staff), or a foul-smelling substance in their hand—

<column-break/>

One should not do so. By doing this, the person experiences daily agitation and suffers from the afflictions of the planets. || 10 ||

**Commentary—** The original verse uses the word 'Gandha' <note>original: "गन्ध" meaning smell or odor</note>. Therefore, in my opinion, asking a question while carrying fragrant substances is auspicious, but asking a question with foul-smelling or pungent substances will be inauspicious. Thus, in this context, it is appropriate to interpret the word 'Gandha' as 'foul odor.'

### || The Results of Giving Gold and Other Offerings ||

->Taking gold, gemstone vessels, and food or drink,<-
->the seeker attains success immediately; of this there is no doubt. || 11 ||<-

**Prose Order:** Taking gold, gemstone vessels, and food and drink; the seeker <term>Prichchhaka: the person asking the astrological question</term> attains success immediately, there is no doubt. || 11 ||

**Richa Commentary—** If the seeker asks a question while bringing gold, silver, gems, etc., as well as food and water, they achieve instant success in their endeavor. || 11 ||

### || The Results of Touching Sacred Ground ||

->When the seeker asks while touching the ground of a garden,<-
->all their desires shall be fulfilled; there is no need for further deliberation here. || 12 ||<-

**Prose Order:** When the seeker asks while touching the ground of a garden, all their success shall manifest. No further thought is required on this matter. || 12 ||

**Richa Commentary—** When the questioner <term>Prashta: another term for the seeker</term> touches the earth of a grove or garden while asking a question, all their works are accomplished; there is no need to doubt this. || 12 ||

->Sitting in a temple, on a riverbank, or in a beautiful place,<-
->when one asks a question, then one attains success. || 13 ||<-

**Prose Order:** When one sits in a temple, a beautiful place, or even on a riverbank and asks, then one attains success. || 13 ||

**Richa Commentary—** When the seeker asks a question while sitting in a temple, by a river, or at any other pleasant location, the seeker's objective is fulfilled. || 13 ||

### || The Results of Forbidden Seating During Inquiry ||

->On dry wood, broken or ruined wood, or near a thicket,<-
->whoever asks in these places, one should predict distress for them. || 14 ||<-

**Prose Order:** On dry wood, wounded (broken), ruined, or in a thicket—whoever asks in these places, one should predict distress for them. || 14 ||

**Richa Commentary—** A seeker who asks a question while sitting on dry wood, a torn or broken wooden seat, or near a bush/thicket should be told that they will encounter distress. One should also not ask questions while sitting on stones or similar hard, uncomfortable surfaces. || 14 ||

### ### The Result of Sitting Comfortably During an Inquiry

> When the inquirer is seated comfortably and positioned in a cardinal direction, there is wonderful success.
> If positioned in an intermediate direction or seated uncomfortably, the success of the work does not occur. || 15 ||

**Prose Order:** If the inquirer is in a cardinal direction and seated comfortably, wonderful success (occurs). If in an intermediate direction or poorly seated, the success of the work does not occur. || 15 ||

**Commentary:** When the inquirer asks a question while sitting comfortably in a correct cardinal direction, then their work is accomplished. When the questioner asks while sitting in the intermediate directions (corners) or on an improper seat, then their work is not accomplished. || 15 ||

**Statement:** There are four cardinal directions: East, West, North, and South. Sitting correctly in any of these is called **standing in the direction** <note>Sanskrit: dikstha</note>. However, sitting in the four corners is called **standing in the intermediate direction** <note>Sanskrit: vidikstha</note>. The measurement of directions is calculated from the seat of the <term>astrologer</term> <note>Sanskrit: daivajña; literally "one who knows the divine/fate"</note>.

Sitting in the correct direction and asking a question while comfortably placed on an excellent seat is fruitful. Asking a question of the astrologer while in the corners or on a forbidden seat results in failure.

### ### Briefly, the Results of Touching Body Parts

> Thumb, ear, face, breast, hand, hair,
> Waist, shoulder, sole of the foot, private parts, head, and cheek.
> If the inquirer touches these or speaks auspicious words,
> Then they certainly attain the desired success. || 16 ||

**Prose Order:** If the inquirer touches the thumb, ear, face, breast, hand, hair, waist, shoulder, sole of the foot, private parts, head, and cheek, or speaks auspicious things, then they certainly attain the desired success. || 16 ||

**Commentary:** When the inquirer, while touching any of these parts—the thumb (of the hands), ear, mouth, breast, hand, hair, waist, sole of the foot, private parts, head, cheek, or lip—asks a question with auspicious speech, then they definitely gather success. || 16 ||

### ### According to Another Opinion

> If one touches the head, face, eyes, or ears,
> One obtains grain, clothing, and gold.
> When a man touches the neck, jaw, or both shoulders,
> Then one should predict his gain through suffering. || 17 ||

**Prose Order:** When a man touches the head, face, eyes, or ears, then he obtains grain and clothing preceded by gold. If (he touches) the neck, jaw, or the pair of shoulders, then one should predict his acquisition through distress. || 17 ||

**Commentary:** Now the result of touching body parts is told according to a different opinion. If the inquirer touches the head, mouth, eyes, or ears, then gold, grain, and similar items are obtained. However, if the neck... <note>The text cuts off here and continues on the next page.</note>

# Compendium of Kerala Prasnashastra
<note>Kerala Prasnashastra is a specialized system of Vedic horary astrology originating from Kerala, focusing on immediate divination.</note>

If the querent touches their chin or shoulders while asking a question, they will encounter failure and sorrow. || 17 ||

->Touching the navel or the belly indicates the attainment of wealth.<-
->Touching the ankles, feet, or knees indicates extreme sorrow.<-
->One who touches the shanks, the waist, or the genitals here<-
->shall obtain a suitable bride easily through good fortune. || 18 ||<-
->Touching the hair leads to lordship; touching fruits and such<-
->is auspicious. Touching grass or the remains of fire (ashes/charcoal)<-
->does not bring success. Touching mud, wood, or (worn) clothing<-
->leads to suffering caused by the planets as well as mental agony. || 19 ||<-

**Prose Order (Sanskrit)** — Touching the belly and navel: attainment of wealth. Touching the ankles, feet, and knees: extreme sorrow. He who touches the shanks, waist, or genitals here: he shall obtain an easily available bride through good fortune. Touching the hair: lordship. Touching fruits etc.: auspicious. Grass or fire-remnants: indeed no success. Touching mud, wood, or cloth: one receives planetary suffering and mental distress. || 18-19 ||

**Commentary** — If the querent asks a question while touching the belly or the navel, the work will be successful. If they ask while touching the ankles, feet, or knees, there is great sorrow. If they touch the shanks, waist, or genitals, they will obtain an excellent bride through a favorable alignment of stars.
One who asks while touching their hair attains lordship (power/prosperity). One who asks while touching fruits or similar items receives auspicious results. One who asks while touching grass or the remains of fire <note>original: "vahnishesam" — refers to charcoal or ash</note> will not see their work succeed. One who asks while touching mud, wood, or (worn-out) clothing receives suffering from the planets <term>Graha-pida: the perceived negative influence of celestial bodies on human affairs</term>. || 18-19 ||

**Note** — Verses 16-17-18-19 appear to be <insert>interpolations</insert> <note>original: "prakshipta" — later additions not part of the original core text</note>, but since they appear in certain manuscripts, they have been included here.

### || Determining the Ascendant (Lagna) via the Letters of the Question ||

->The 'A' group indicates Leo; the 'Ka' group indicates Aries and Scorpio.<-
->The 'Ca' group indicates Libra and Taurus; the 'Ta' group indicates Gemini and Virgo. || 1 ||<-
->The 'Ta' group indicates Sagittarius and Pisces; the 'Pa' group indicates Aquarius and Capricorn.<-
->The 'Ya' and 'Sha' groups indicate Cancer; thus one should declare the Lagna by the letters of the words. || 2 ||<-

**Prose Order (Sanskrit)** — In the 'A' group: Leo Lagna. In the 'Ka' group: Aries and Scorpio. In the 'Ca' group: Taurus and Libra. In the 'Ta' group: Gemini and Virgo. In the 'Ta' group: Sagittarius and Pisces. In the 'Pa' group: Capricorn and Aquarius. In the 'Ya' group and 'Sha' group: Cancer. Thus, one should state the Lagna by the letters of the words. || 1-2 ||

**Commentary** — To determine the Ascendant <term>Lagna: the zodiac sign rising on the eastern horizon at the time of the query</term>, one uses the first letter uttered by the querent's mouth, or the result...

<page-num>69</page-num>
<header>Latter Half</header>

Based on the first letter of the name of a flower or similar object: if it belongs to the 'A-group' (vowels), the rising sign is Leo. In the 'Ka-group' (gutturals), the signs are Aries and Scorpio. In the 'Cha-group' (palatals), Taurus and Libra. In the 'Ta-group' (cerebrals), Gemini and Virgo. In the 'Ta-group' (dentals), Sagittarius and Pisces. In the 'Pa-group' (labials), Capricorn and Aquarius. If the first letter is from the 'Ya-group' (semivowels) or 'Sha-group' (sibilants), the rising sign is Cancer. || 1-2 ||

## || Knowledge of the Group Lords ||

->The Sun, Mars, Venus, Mercury, Jupiter, Saturn, and the Moon are the lords of the groups in order.<-
->Among these, starting from Mars, the odd letters correspond to odd signs and even letters to even signs.<-
->One should predict the results from that rising sign based on the planetary combinations to be described.<-
->One should determine the lords of the rising signs from the letters uttered in the question.<- || 3-4 ||

**Connection of Words:** The Sun, Mars, Venus, Mercury, Jupiter, Saturn, and the Moon are the lords of the groups in sequence. Among those (lords) starting with Mars, the odd letters result in an odd-numbered rising sign, and even letters result in an even-numbered rising sign. One should indicate the fruit (result) from that rising sign using the planetary combinations to be mentioned. Thus, one should assign the lords of the rising signs based on the letters spoken during the inquiry. || 3-4 ||

**Commentary:** The eight groups—'A, Ka, Cha, Ta, Ta, Pa, Ya, Sha'—are ruled respectively by the Sun, Mars, Venus, Mercury, Jupiter, Saturn, and the Moon. <note>This is the 'Ashta-Varga' or eight-fold classification of the alphabet used in Vedic divination.</note> Generally, there are five letters in each group. If the first letter of the group is an odd-numbered letter <note>e.g., 1st, 3rd, or 5th in the sequence</note>, then for the five non-luminary planets (Mars etc.), the rising sign will be an odd-numbered zodiac sign. If it is an even-numbered letter, the rising sign will be an even-numbered sign. The Sun and the Moon rule only one sign each <note>Leo and Cancer respectively</note>, so their sign remains constant. In the rising sign determined this way, one should calculate the results by placing the planets according to the current almanac <term>Panchang: a five-limb Hindu calendar used to find auspicious times and planetary positions</term>. || 3-4 ||

->|| (1) Chart for finding the Rising Sign in the Banner Income (A-group) ||<-

| A/Aa | I/Ee | U/Oo | E/Ai | O/Au | Letters of A-group |
| :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- |
| Leo | Leo | Leo | Leo | Leo | Sun's Sign |

| :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- |

<note>The "Income" (Aaya) categories like Banner, Smoke, Lion, and Dog refer to a mathematical formula used in traditional Indian architecture and astrology to determine the character of a direction or name.</note>

## Chaukhamba Surbharati Prakashan
### Varanasi
<note>Varanasi, also known as Benares or Kashi, is the traditional center of Hindu learning and publishing in India.</note>

<term>Kerala Prashna Shastra Sangraha: A collection of teachings on the Kerala school of horary astrology.</term>
<term>Richa: The name of the specific commentary; literally means a sacred verse or hymn.</term>
<term>Tikakar: The author of a commentary (Tika) who explains the original cryptic Sanskrit verses.</term>
<term>Acharya Guru Prasad Gaur: The name of the scholar who translated and annotated this edition.</term>
<term>Chaukhamba Surbharati Prakashan: A famous publishing house specializing in Sanskrit and classical Indian texts.</term>
<term>Varanasi: One of the world's oldest continually inhabited cities and a hub for Vedic scholarship.</term>
<term>Spirituality must be understood: The motto found in the publisher's emblem (original: "Adhyatmam praviditavyam").</term>
<term>Jyotish: The traditional Indian system of astronomy and astrology.</term>
<term>Prashna Shastra: The science of "Questions," where a horoscope is cast for the exact moment a person asks a question to find the answer.</term>

<summary>This is the title page for a scholarly edition of a Kerala-style astrological manual, featuring a Hindi commentary by Acharya Guru Prasad Gaur and published by the renowned Chaukhamba press in Varanasi.</summary>
